#5258. Airdrop Trap? XRP Community Gets Targeted by 11.5 Million Scam Posts

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

The XRP community has been hit by a massive wave of suspected scam activity, with about 11.5 million posts targeting XRP users through fake reward and airdrop promises over the last 30 days.

The figure comes from an analysis using X's API that tracked posts specifically targeting the XRP community.

In an X post, the XRP Ledger Foundation's director of community, Hussein Zangana (Vet), mentioned doing some analytics with X's API. He revealed the outcome: over 11.5 million posts targeted the XRP community to scam its members with reward and airdrop promises.

"Doing some X analytics with their API. In the last 30 days about 11.5 million (!!) posts were made targeting specifically the XRP community to scam them with reward and airdrop promises," Vet wrote. The figure reflects the volume of posts identified by the analysis as targeting XRP users with fraudulent reward and airdrop promises.

Doing some X analytics with their API. In the last 30 days about 11.5 million (!!) posts were made targeting specifically the XRP community to scam them with reward and airdrop promises.

XRP is seeing increased interest, with scammers wanting to capitalize on this by enticing unsuspecting users with promises of free tokens, rewards, and exclusive airdrop opportunities.

The intent is to lure users into connecting their wallets to malicious websites, signing harmful transactions, or revealing sensitive wallet information.

XRP continues to gain popularity and adoption, as reflected in a recent RWA report.

According to a recent report by RWA Foundation, the XRP Ledger holds $642.64 million in distributed real-world assets across 2,497 holders, per recent RWA.xyz data. Franklin Templeton is the largest distributed platform. Another $5.21 billion is recorded on the ledger as represented assets, which RWA.xyz counts separately.

Distributed RWA value on XRPL grew 28.81% in the 30 days to November 6, 2026, from $498.90 million. Franklin Templeton's sgBENJI accounts for all of the growth: its XRPL balance rose by about $144 million while other distributed assets were flat. The number of holders grew 0.20%, and represented value fell 0.38%. XRPL carries 456 RWAs.

XRPL holds $926.60 million in stablecoins across 75,840 holders, down 22.91% over 30 days. Stablecoin transfers totaled $9.30 billion over the same window, up 63.56%.

Bitcoin fell to $68,617, its lowest level in three months. The sell-off forced out hundreds of millions of dollars in leveraged bets within an hour, and XRP dropped to the $1 mark. Bitcoin is down 6.6% over 24 hours and 9.5% over the week. Ethereum fell 6.0% to $1,926.48 and has lost 13.7% in seven days. About $60 billion was wiped off the total crypto market value in four hours, according to reports.

The drop started when Bitcoin broke below $72,000. It then fell through $70,000 and $69,000, and each break set off more forced selling.

Liquidation figures differ by source, but every count shows a sharp spike:

Most of the losses fell on traders betting on higher prices. When the market moves against them, their positions are closed automatically, and that selling adds to the drop.

XRP is trading at $1.00, down 4.5% in 24 hours and 17.5% over the past week. That is the steepest weekly loss among the top 10 coins. Its market value has shrunk to about $63.8 billion.

The token was near $1.05 to $1.06 earlier in the week. It is now sitting right on the $1 level, which traders watch closely as a psychological floor.

Bitcoinâs market value has fallen to about $1.38 trillion and it now sits about 46% below its record high of around $126,198, set in October 2025.

On the chart, Bitcoin keeps posting lower highs. Buyers first need to win back $70,000 and then $72,000, the level where the breakdown started. Until then, buying into the drop carries high risk while prices are still falling.

#5256. Ethereum – What BitMine’s $6.3B loss reveals about building treasury around ETH

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The SEC has released BitMine’s Form 10-K.

According to the filing, the firm suffered a $6.3 billion net loss during the fiscal year ending August 31, 2026.

As the fiscal year runs from September 2025 to August 2026, the reported loss largely reflects Ethereum’s price decline following the October 2025 crash, which pushed ETH from a peak above $4k toward $2k.

In essence, much of the $6.3 billion loss came from unrealized losses on its ETH holdings.

The timing of the filing is also notable. BitMine has recently disclosed that it holds 6.07 million ETH, 216 BTC, and $475 million in cash and marketable securities. It also holds a stake in Beast Industries and Eightco. Altogether, its crypto, cash, securities, and moonshot investments were valued at $14.3 billion.

This demonstrates that the company has a large asset portfolio despite the large amount of paper loss they face. However, with ETH prices under pressure, continued accumulation also increases its exposure to further unrealized losses.

This brings attention to the recent thesis that Vitalik Buterin put forward regarding a new ‘store of value’ story. As previously covered by AMBCrypto, Buterin’s thesis aims to shift focus away from Ethereum’s deflationary model and instead focus on real demand for the asset.

Simply put, instead of relying mainly on reduced supply to drive value, the thesis puts greater emphasis on real demand and network adoption.

However, with BitMine alone losing $6.3 billion, it becomes an issue of whether the “demand-driven SOV” story holds when even the largest institutions holding ETH are losing significant sums.

This raises a key question: Does this put Ethereum’s long-term store-of-value narrative at risk?

For BitMine to get back into the green, Ethereum [ETH] would need a rally of nearly 50%.

However, the broader market signals suggest that recovery may not come easy. E

thereum has already underperformed Bitcoin by over 2x in Q4, falling nearly 25%. If the trend continues, the $2,000 level could come under pressure, pushing BitMine’s 6 million ETH holdings deeper into unrealized losses and adding more strain to its balance sheet.

Notably, the risk of holding such a large ETH position may also be starting to rise.

According to on-chain data, another 1,000 ETH worth $2.03 million was sold by one of the Ethereum Foundation’s wallets. Over the past week, large holders have sold a total of 17,800 ETH worth about $40 million. Meanwhile, BlackRock moved another $151 million worth of ETH to Coinbase Prime.

In essence, bulls are still not fully buying into Bitmine’s demand-driven SoV thesis.

Against this backdrop, Bitmine’s $6.3 billion in unrealized losses could put upward pressure on this scenario. As shown in the post above, BMNR is being seen by the market as a major catalyst for Ethereum becoming a SoV asset.

However, this narrative could face a reality check if ETH continues to underperform and Bitmine’s losses keep growing.

Thus, while BitMine’s 6 million ETH holdings suggest strong institutional demand, its recent SEC filing also demonstrates the dangers of such heavy accumulation, putting Ethereum’s long-term SoV story to the test.

BitMine’s $6.3 billion loss shows the risks of holding such a large amount of ETH as prices fall.

While its 6 million ETH holdings signal strong demand, growing losses could challenge Ethereum’s long-term SoV story.

#5255. $380 Million in Shiba Inu (SHIB) Held on Exchanges: Will It Bounce Here?

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

According to data from the analytical platform CryptoQuant, around 86.04 trillion SHIB are currently held on exchanges, worth approximately $382.87 million.

$380 million is somewhat of a baseline in terms of Shiba Inu holdings. The asset has been struggling for almost a year now, and $380 million is the whole supply available for sale. It's important to keep in mind that if someone triggers an 86 trillion Shiba Inu sellout, that batch won't cost $380 million. We'll be lucky to see one-third of it.

Shiba Inu's liquidity is thin if we measure it against institutional appetites. Most of the holders are regular retail investors.

The netflow remains negative, with around 45.5 billion SHIB leaving exchanges over the last 24 hours. Outflows accounted for 333.7 billion SHIB compared with 326.2 billion in inflows.

Inflows and outflows most likely reflect usual trading and investment activities: investors constantly move funds into self-custody and sell them. As we can see, the net value of those funds barely reaches $200,000, making those operations irrelevant to the big picture.

SHIB is currently trading at $0.00000444, below all major moving averages.

Shiba Inu mostly followed the general uptrend, but tumbled fairly quickly due to the general reversal of the cryptocurrency market. The ascending trendline that held the price since July was broken in mid-October, which opened the way toward more losses.

The 200 EMA acted as strong resistance that SHIB was unable to break. The downtrend is much stronger than the bullish reversal attempts SHIB made in October.

RSI is moving around 37, which is somewhat bearish, but not oversold yet. Volume should also surge substantially for any reversal, while right now it sits at its lowest level in months.

The bounce is unlikely to occur here naturally unless SHIB lands on $0.00000413, which was the reversal point back in July, where some buy orders might be concentrated.

Whether buyers can defend the $0.00000413 level or not is the main test for the asset right now. If SHIB violates it, there's no clear support on the chart below, unfortunately.

#5254. Is The Outlook Outage A Cyber Attack?

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Microsoft says the global outage hitting Outlook, Teams and other Microsoft 365 services was not caused by a cyber attack or a surge in users. The company said some of the servers running these services stopped working, leaving the remaining servers overloaded. The disruption began early Friday Australian time, or late Thursday night in India, and has now lasted more than 10 hours. Microsoft said its latest fixes are not working as expected and it still cannot say when full service will return. Some servers are back online and a few regions have partly recovered, but restored servers froze again once users logged back in. Affected services include Outlook email, Teams, SharePoint, Microsoft 365 Copilot, Intune and Defender security tools. Downdetector logged about 120,000 Outlook outage reports worldwide in the early hours. In Australia, government agencies, councils and Queensland Health were among those hit. It is Microsoft 365’s third major outage this year, after disruptions in January and August.

#5253. $284 Million of Shiba Inu (SHIB) Held by Top Korean Exchange

As the broad crypto market continues to face a prolonged downturn, it appears that South Korea's largest cryptocurrency exchange, Upbit, has seized the opportunity to stack up Shiba Inu at lower prices.

Latest data from blockchain intelligence firm Arkham shows that Upbit has recorded a sharp increase in its Shiba Inu holdings as the exchange appears to have continued accumulating the asset.

Despite the market’s persistent downturn, Upbit has expanded its Shiba Inu holdings to hit 64.16 trillion Shiba Inu (SHIB) tokens as of November 11, 2026.

At Shiba Inu’s current price, the exchange’s total Shiba Inu holdings are now worth about $284 million, according to data from Arkham Intelligence, positioning the firm as one of Shiba Inu’s largest holders.

Further details showcased on the platform show that the tokens are held in wallets labeled as belonging to Upbit, suggesting continued accumulation.

Recent reports show that an August 2026 audit also confirmed that its SHIB reserves exceeded user liabilities, further indicating its strong exposure to the leading meme token.

Apart from Upbit, the data further shows that other large companies also hold large amounts of SHIB, but Upbit remains the largest SHIB holding entity among them.

Robinhood's largest cold wallet holds about 39.27 trillion SHIB, worth about $174.37 million at SHIB’s current price. This represents about 3.93% of the asset’s supply.

Although Upbit stands as SHIB’s largest holding entity, the data show that the SHIB burn address remains the largest holder, as it contains about 410.44 trillion SHIB, representing about 41.04% of SHIB’s total supply.

#5252. Why Hyperliquid’s 17% price drop puts HYPE’s $69 support at risk

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Over the last week, Hyperliquid [HYPE] has dropped 17%, trading at $72.49 as of writing. Despite bullish exchange listings on South Korea’s Upbit and Bithumb on the 10th of November, the token has struggled to regain ground after falling below $87-$89. The latest price action suggests that sellers have continued to dictate the direction of the market.

At the same time, selling pressure has been rising from several directions. On the 31st of October, two large HYPE holders sold $109 million in tokens after Intercontinental Exchange (ICE) released a more relaxed regulatory statement about perpetual Futures.

Then, on the 8th of November, Binance reopened HYPE withdrawals, giving holders access to their tokens again after the exchange’s security breach in October.

HYPE’s weekly chart has remained under pressure over the past seven days, with price breaking below the 50% Fibonacci level and moving into the 61.8% retracement zone. At the time of writing, HYPE was trading around $72.49 and had officially entered the golden pocket.

The indicators also pointed to the market cooling as the weekly RSI stood at 51.07 and continued to trend lower, suggesting that the bullish momentum seen in previous months was beginning to fade.

Furthermore, OBV had declined from its August-September highs, clearly displaying the difficulty buyers had faced in matching the volume behind recent selling.

The daily chart also offered very little evidence of a reversal happening anytime soon. HYPE remained below the 23.6% Fibonacci retracement and is hovering close to its recent swing low at $70.44. An RSI of 35.35 pointed towards continued bearish momentum.

On the other hand, OBV’s recent uptick suggested that buyers’ interest had peaked; however, sustained buying would be needed for price to have any meaningful recovery.

A break below $70.44 could shift attention towards the weekly $69.03 retracement. If buyers defend this area, some relief could be seen, potentially giving HYPE room to attempt a recovery. However, if this support gives way, the next downside target sits at the weekly Fibonacci level of $61.16.

#5251. Bitcoin CPI Reaction: 10 of 13 Cool Inflation Reports Triggered an Hourly Gain – Can Inflation Data Reverse the Downtrend?

Bitcoin CPI reaction: October CPI lands at 8:30 a.m. ET, core seen at 0.22%. Cool prints lifted BTC in the first hour 10 times out of 13.

The U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics publishes October’s consumer price index (CPI) on Tuesday, November 10, at 8:30 a.m. in New York, or 13:30 UTC. The Bitcoin CPI reaction that follows could set the tone for the week. The CPI is the main U.S. measure of inflation, and it will shape the Federal Reserve’s next rate decision.

Economists expect core prices, which leave out food and energy, to have risen about 0.22% in the month. That would be a slowdown from September’s 0.33%.

Economists expect growth in the core CPI to have moderated in October to around 0.22%, though the full range of estimates is broad enough to round down to 0.1% or round up to 0.3%.

Due to the government shutdown in Oct 2025, there's no tidy year-earlier figure. pic.twitter.com/CFM7ikw8fO

— Nick Timiraos (@NickTimiraos) November 9, 2026

Bitcoin trades near $74,400, still close to Sunday’s two-month low of $73,510.

CPI days move crypto. We checked Bitcoin’s first hour after the last 24 releases. When core inflation came in at 0.2% or lower, BTC rose in that hour 10 times out of 13. When it came in at 0.3% or higher, it rose only 4 times out of 10.

So which way will today’s number push Bitcoin?

The forecasts point to a calmer month. Seventeen banks and research firms surveyed by The Wall Street Journal see a median rise of 0.25% for headline CPI and 0.22% for core. Journal reporter Nick Timiraos posted the table on X.

That spread matters. The government reports CPI rounded to one decimal place. With forecasts this spread out, the core reading could print anywhere from 0.1% to 0.3%. A 0.3% would match September’s pace and read as inflation that is not cooling.

The yearly comparison will also be messy. The government shutdown in October 2025 meant no CPI was published for that month, so there is no clean year-over-year figure.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best Low-Cap Crypto

This is the last CPI report the Federal Reserve will see before its December 8-9 meeting. Traders already expect a rate hike: on Tuesday morning, Polymarket gives an 86.5% chance of a 0.25-point increase and 11.5% for no change.

Higher rates tend to weigh on Bitcoin. They make cash and bonds pay more, which pulls money away from assets that pay no interest, like crypto.

A cool reading could shave those odds and give Bitcoin room to bounce. A hot one would lock in the hike.

DISCOVER: Fed Meeting Today: Traders Price a 95% Hold and a 79% December Hike. What Does It Mean for Crypto?

We measured the Bitcoin CPI reaction on Binance. We took the 24 CPI reports published since October 2024 and measured Bitcoin’s move in the hour after each 8:30 a.m. release.

The pattern is a lean, not a rule. The biggest drop came on February 12, 2025, when core rose 0.4% and BTC lost 1.9% in an hour. Last month’s 0.3% reading cost it 1.4%.

So far it has. BTC trades at $74,398 on Binance, about 1.2% above Sunday’s low.

On the daily chart, Bitcoin sits below both of its exponential moving averages (EMAs), averages that weight recent prices more. The 200-day EMA is at $76,481 and the 50-day EMA at $79,128.

For anyone tracking the Bitcoin CPI reaction, the first hour after 8:30 a.m. is the one to watch. In 20 of the last 24 reports, Bitcoin ended the next 24 hours moving in the same direction as in that first hour.

#5250. JuvrixAI Kicks Off $JUX Presale on NEAR Protocol to Bring Spending Limits to Autonomous AI Agents

Disclaimer: The below article is sponsored, and the views in it do not represent those of ZyCrypto. Readers should conduct independent research before taking any actions related to the project mentioned in this piece. This article should not be regarded as investment advice.

The no-code platform gives every AI agent its own wallet and on-chain rules, so agents can act on their own without putting their owners’ funds at risk

JuvrixAI, a no-code platform for building AI agents on NEAR Protocol, has started the presale of its utility token, $JUX. The project tackles a central question of the AI agent era: how to let agents act autonomously, including spending money, while their owners stay in control.

AI agents become truly useful when they can finish tasks: finding the data, choosing the tool, paying for the service. But autonomy and payments create a trade-off.

Giving an agent direct access to a wallet or card means full spending power and full risk. Approving every payment manually keeps funds safe but removes the autonomy that makes agents valuable. And since agents can be manipulated through prompt injection, telling an agent to “be careful” is not a reliable safeguard.

Because the contract enforces the rules, a tricked agent still cannot exceed its limits or pay an unapproved recipient.

Purchase $JUX Token on Presale: https://juvrixai.com/presale

Agent operators can stake $JUX to earn an on-chain verified trust badge that merchants can check before accepting a payment, helping services distinguish accountable agents from unknown ones.

$JUX is used to pay fees at a discount, unlock tiered discounts on agent execution, vote on templates and roadmap priorities, stake for verified badges, and gain priority testnet access.

#5249. Dogecoin Core GitHub Billing Issue Resolved: Top Dev Hints at Uncertainty

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

In a recent post on social media, Dogecoin engineer Michi Lumin noted that a GitHub billing issue affecting Dogecoin Core has been addressed, although uncertainty remains on how the expenses will be handled in the coming months.

Lumin disclosed that Dogecoin Core's GitHub account ran into billing issues, which she resolved by personally paying the arrears and then switching the billing to herself.

This situation follows major Dogecoin Core developer Patrick Lodder's absence on the 'core' front, with Lumin describing him as missing in action (MIA). However, Lumin clarified that this reflected her own personal experience and speculated that others might have remained in contact with him.

"So @dogecoin , Pat Lodder has gone MIA for months on the Core front (at least to ME. If others have talked to him, great!), and the Core GitHub has run into some billing issues. I've paid the arrears personally and switched billing to myself," Lumin wrote.

@Doge_France @dogecoin as far as i know it was fees for CI workers or actions that they bill/meter. I've been reachable but that's a whole other kettle of bees around here. Either way, it's paid right now, so we'll see how next month goes and i'll address it then if i need to.

The update from Lumin prompted a response from Dogecoin community member 'Doge France', who offered to contribute financially if assistance was needed, saying that keeping the Core GitHub infrastructure operational should be a priority.

Responding to the offer, Lumin explained that the outstanding charges appeared to be related to GitHub Actions or continuous integration (CI) workers, whose usage is billed according to consumption.

Although the immediate payment has been completed, Lumin stopped short of confirming a long-term funding arrangement for the Core GitHub. The developer said the next billing cycle would provide a clearer picture, which will be addressed if necessary.

"As far as i know, it was fees for CI workers or actions that they bill/meter.  I've been reachable but that's a whole other kettle of bees around here. Either way, it's paid right now, so we'll see how next month goes and i'll address it then if i need to," Lumin wrote.

The Dogecoin Foundation, a non-profit organization for which Lumin serves as principal engineer, supports open-source development, advocacy, and community work for Dogecoin and relies primarily on community contributions for its funding.

In the past month, Dogecoin announced a consolidation across its ecosystem with the launch of Dogecoin Labs, which unites Dogecoin’s product teams, technology and applications.

#5245. XRP Price Plunges to $1.05 After a Hot CPI Report: Will the Support Stand?

Ripple XRP fell to $1.05 after a hot CPI report. After 7 of the last 10 hot reports, XRP was higher a day later. Can $1.05 hold?

Ripple XRP fell to $1.05 on Tuesday within five minutes of the U.S. October consumer price index (CPI) report, which showed XRP USD price rising faster than economists expected.

XRP crypto traded at $1.0797 when the report came out at 8:30 a.m. in New York, or 13:30 UTC. By 13:35 UTC it had touched $1.0500 on Binance, a 2.8% drop and its lowest price since August 19. It has since bounced to about $1.07. Bitcoin fell with it, dropping to $73,525 in the same five minutes.

The drop puts XRP back on a level it has tested before. Between late June and mid-August, XRP traded around $1.05 for weeks. The level held the first time and broke the second.

So will the support stand this time, and what do the CPI numbers tell us?

BREAKING: October CPI inflation rises +0.4% month-over-month, above expectations of +0.3%.

Core CPI inflation rose +0.3%, also above expectations of +0.2%.

BLS will not be releasing year-over-year figures due to the October 2025 government shutdown.

— The Kobeissi Letter (@KobeissiLetter) November 10, 2026

Prices rose faster than forecast in October. The Bureau of Labor Statistics said consumer prices rose 0.4% in the month, against the 0.3% economists expected.

Core prices, which leave out food and energy because they swing the most, rose 0.3%. The forecast was 0.2%. Core inflation has now run at about 0.3% for three months in a row.

The report is missing one usual figure. The bureau did not publish a yearly inflation rate because it never collected prices in October 2025, during the government shutdown. That leaves the monthly numbers to carry the whole report.

Those monthly numbers matter for the Federal Reserve. Fed Governor Christopher Waller said in a speech this morning that he already leans toward a 0.25 percentage point rate hike in December. If core CPI came in around 0.3% again, he said, a 0.5 point hike “should be considered”.

Core CPI did come in at 0.3%. Before the release, traders saw almost no chance of a hike that large. Higher rates make cash and bonds pay more, which tends to pull money out of risky assets like XRP.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Next Crypto to Hit 1 Dollar

The first move after a hot report has often faded. We measured XRP’s price on Binance in the 24 hours after each of the last 24 CPI reports, from October 2024 to October 2026.

Ten of those reports had core inflation of 0.3% or higher, like today’s. After seven of them, XRP was higher 24 hours later. The median move was a 1.4% gain.

The record is weaker this year. Of the four hot reports in 2026, XRP was higher a day later only twice. After the last one, on October 14, it fell 4.4% in 24 hours.

The difference this time is the Fed. A hot report that only confirms a small hike is easier for traders to shrug off. One that puts a larger hike on the table, as Waller laid out, gives sellers more to work with.

EXPLORE: XRP Price Forecast: Ripple at Risk of Losing Key Support, $1 Next?

The level has a history this year. XRP has spent two long stretches around $1.05 since June.

Today’s low of $1.0500 sits right on that zone. The first test of a level often holds. Each new test tends to leave fewer buyers there.

XRP trades at about $1.07 on Binance after the CPI drop, down 2.8% in 24 hours and 17% in a week. It is 71% below its July 2025 record of $3.65.

On the daily chart, where each candle covers one day, XRP sits well below both of its exponential moving averages (EMAs), averages that give recent prices more weight. The 50-day EMA stands at $1.294 and the 200-day EMA at $1.350.

For Ripple XRP, the first test of $1.05 has held so far. The daily close decides it: a close above $1.05 keeps the support alive, while a close below it opens the way to $1.

#5242. Bitcoin Core Breaks Silence on Three Patched Security Vulnerabilities

Bitcoin Core developers have revealed three key security vulnerabilities, including a high-severity flaw that could potentially disrupt the mining capabilities of the Bitcoin network, preventing miners from creating new blocks.

As revealed in security advisories published on Nov. 10, the vulnerabilities impacted various elements of the Bitcoin Core software that powers many nodes underpinning the world's leading cryptocurrency.

One of the vulnerabilities, titled CVE-2025-62861, was rated as a high-severity issue due to its potential effect on Bitcoin miners.

The bug has the potential to create problems after a blockchain reorganization, which is when previously accepted blocks are replaced by the network with an alternative chain.

More precisely, the issue could cause an inconsistent state in the node's mempool, where all unconfirmed transactions are stored.

The vulnerability impacted Bitcoin Core software versions 28.0-28.2 and 29.0-29.1.

It was first reported on Sept. 26, 2025, by security researcher Niklas Goegge.

Beyond the mining vulnerability, Bitcoin Core developers have announced two further security problems, rated medium severity.

The first one (CVE-2025-62859) could enable malicious network participants to slow down Bitcoin block processing through an exploit that involves changes in transaction witness data.

Under some circumstances, it could make an affected node wait for up to 10 minutes before processing a block.

Moreover, such an attack could be repeated indefinitely on the node when the latter is running in specific configurations.

The vulnerability was found by Eugene Siegel in April 2025.

The second vulnerability (CVE-2025-62860) involved Bitcoin nodes that operate on the anonymous Tor network.

In fact, the Bitcoin Core developers revealed that thousands of reachable Bitcoin mainnet nodes had been granting permissions for incoming Tor connections.

Nevertheless, the issue did not affect nodes that do not use the optional whitelist configuration.

The three vulnerabilities are not new discoveries.

In fact, all of the issues were already fixed by the Bitcoin Core developers in October 2025.

The fixes were integrated into both Bitcoin Core 30.0, which was released on Oct. 10, and Bitcoin Core 29.2, rolled out on Oct. 14.

The high-severity mining vulnerability was also patched in Bitcoin Core 28.3.

The delay in the disclosure of the vulnerabilities was in line with the Bitcoin Core security policy, which usually calls for developers to wait at least two weeks after the end of life of the last affected software version before publishing details of medium- or high-severity vulnerabilities.

#5240. Onramp Launches Business Bitcoin Accounts With Team Withdrawal Approvals

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Onramp has launched a business Bitcoin account with named users, role permissions and withdrawal approvals. A separate Treasury tier adds multi-institution custody. The distinction matters because team account controls and the underlying signing arrangement are different parts of security.

For a company buying Bitcoin, the first operational question arrives quickly: who is allowed to move it? A purchase approved by the board still needs a process for everyday access, record keeping and changes in personnel.

Onramp’s new offering addresses that administrative layer. Its significance is practical: making a corporate Bitcoin position something a finance team can govern through defined responsibilities, rather than treating account access as one person’s private task.

The company’s November 10 announcement says Onramp Business supports up to three named users, role-based permissions, withdrawal approval policies and an audit trail. The entry account has no monthly charge and a stated 0.85% trading fee, with assets held by a qualified custodian.

Those features are intended to separate initiating a payment from authorizing it. In a well-designed company process, the person preparing a transfer need not have unilateral authority to complete it. An audit trail can then help explain who requested and approved an action if it is later questioned.

That does not remove the need for an internal policy. A system can enforce permissions only after the business decides who should have them, which transfers require review and what happens when an employee leaves.

Onramp’s current product page lists its Treasury tier from $375 a month, with a 0.65% trading fee. It includes vaults in the company’s name and a structure requiring two of three institutions to move Bitcoin. The page labels custom approval rules as forthcoming.

The basic account should therefore not be described as already including every Treasury feature. Likewise, a planned feature should not appear in a list of capabilities customers can use today.

Separating those tiers also makes pricing easier to interpret. A monthly service charge and a trading fee pay for different things; the cost comparison depends on the amount held, transaction frequency and the control arrangements a company actually needs.

Bitcoin.org’s wallet-security guidance explains that multisignature arrangements can require several independent signatures before funds move. The general idea is to make compromise of one device or authority insufficient to authorize a transaction.

Operational approvals in an application and cryptographic signatures are not automatically the same safeguard. A finance team needs to understand both: what its own users can approve, and which parties ultimately control the keys needed to spend the assets.

NewsBTC has covered related approaches through Sygnum’s shared-key Bitcoin lending arrangement and AnchorWatch’s custody and insurance offering. Those services address different use cases, but each makes the allocation of control central to the product.

The less visible test is what happens when normal operations break down. A director may leave, an authorized employee may become unavailable, or a service provider may be unable to complete its part. A company considering custody needs a documented recovery process as well as a normal withdrawal process.

Extra checks can introduce friction, so a treasury also needs to distinguish assets reserved for long-term holding from assets it may need to move quickly. Strong controls are most useful when they fit the business’s actual payment and liquidity requirements.

Onramp’s launch offers another route for putting those responsibilities into a managed service. Whether it suits a particular company depends on the contractual terms, key structure, costs and operating requirements, rather than the existence of a Bitcoin account alone.

written by the News Desk and edited by Samuel Rae.

#5239. Ripple-Backed Evernorth Plunges 40% as Insiders Move to Sell 73.7 Million Shares

Ripple-backed XRP treasury company Evernorth Holdings (XRPN) is under extreme pressure as its shares have declined by over 40% while big investors like Ripple Labs move closer to potentially selling millions of shares.

Earlier this week, Evernorth filed a prospectus with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC), indicating that the XRP-focused company has registered a whopping 73,688,162 Class A shares for potential resale by existing shareholders.

Evernorth's prospectus comes as the XRP-focused company is enduring a brutal stock market debut. XRPN began trading on Nasdaq on Oct. 12.

On Nov. 10, Evernorth's stock price dropped 23.54%. XRPN ended the day at $7.96, compared to $10.41 in the previous trading session. Moreover, about 1.39 million shares were traded, which is a significant jump from about 369,000 shares on Nov. 9.

So far, Evernorth's share price has declined by around 48% from its closing price on Oct. 28 ($15.26). The magnitude of the decline is even greater if we consider the high share price on its debut day ($19.39). XRPN's stock price is down by around 59% from that high.

Some of the biggest names and companies linked to the XRP ecosystem are shareholders who have registered shares for potential sale, as per the prospectus filed with the SEC.

For instance, the biggest shareholder is Arrington XRP Capital Fund, which was a sponsor of the merger between Evernorth and Armada Acquisition Corp. II. It has about 33.82 million shares registered for possible resale.

Similarly, the second-largest shareholder is Ripple Labs, which has about 18.14 million shares registered. Then there are various entities linked to SBI Holdings, a big Japanese financial institution. They have 11.79 million shares registered, as per the prospectus filed with the SEC.

The Larsen Lam Children's Remainder Trust, which is linked to Ripple co-founder Chris Larsen, has about 7.15 million shares registered for possible sale.

Other names mentioned in the filing include Pantera Capital, GSR Markets Switzerland and Kraken parent firm Payward.

It is important to understand that the fact that they've registered shares for sale doesn't mean that these investors have sold their holdings or necessarily plan to sell them.

For example, Ripple, the Arrington-sponsored entity and a Larsen-linked entity are all subject to six-month lock-up agreements following the completion of Evernorth's business combination on Oct. 9.

These lock-up agreements are typically set to expire on April 9, 2027, with some exceptions.

#5238. Why Is XRP Bleeding More Than Bitcoin, Ether, and Solana in November Sell-Off? Analyst Cites 3 Brutal Reasons

While Bitcoin, Ethereum, and Solana have also come under bearish pressure during November’s broader crypto market sell-off, XRP is taking a particularly brutal beating, leaving investors wondering why the token is underperforming its biggest rivals. According to prominent analyst EGRAG Crypto, the weakness is no coincidence, pointing to three key factors that could explain XRP’s sharper decline and signal why the token may continue struggling as bearish sentiment grips the market.

Crypto analyst EGRAG Crypto has highlighted XRP’s worsening performance against Bitcoin (BTC), Ethereum (ETH), and Solana (SOL) amid November’s broader cryptocurrency market drawdown. In a Nov. 10 post on X, the pundit noted the striking difference in losses since November 4, showing that Ripple’s XRP has suffered the steepest decline among the four major cryptocurrencies.

According to EGRAG Crypto’s figures, XRP has plunged 23.4% since November 4, significantly more than ETH’s 18.2% decline and SOL’s 17% drop. BTC has demonstrated greater resilience, losing just 10.4% over the same period.

The figures raise an important question: Why is XRP bleeding considerably more than its major competitors? EGRAG Crypto has pointed to three possible factors behind the token’s disproportionate weakness, offering a closer look at the selling pressure weighing on the Ripple-linked token.

The first factor is leveraged long liquidations, which EGRAG assigns a 30% probability. According to the analyst, forced liquidations of bullish positions, combined with weak buying demand, could be accelerating XRP’s fall. When leveraged traders are forced to close their positions during a downturn, the resulting selling pressure can trigger further losses and deepen an existing market correction.

The second possibility, which carries a 20% probability, is selling by early XRP holders. EGRAG suggests that longtime investors, often referred to as OGs, could be taking profits and rotating their capital into other cryptocurrencies. Such activity could increase selling pressure on XRP, particularly if large holders decide to reduce their exposure amid uncertain market conditions.

Finally, EGRAG assigns the highest probability, 50%, to what the expert describes as market manipulation or a deliberate liquidity hunt. Under this scenario, sharp price movements could be designed to trigger stop-loss orders and force weaker traders out of their positions before a potential recovery. The analyst believes this could explain why XRP is experiencing disproportionately heavy selling compared with other major cryptocurrencies.

Notably, these percentages represent EGRAG Crypto’s personal probability-based assessment rather than independently verified evidence of the causes behind XRP’s decline.

Beyond identifying the possible reasons behind XRP’s sharper decline, EGRAG Crypto has also highlighted several important price levels that could determine the token’s next move. The analyst identified the $1.00-$1.04 range as a critical stabilization zone, suggesting that holding this area could help XRP establish a floor amid the ongoing selling pressure.

On the upside, EGRAG believes bulls must first reclaim the $1.15 level before targeting $1.25. A recovery above these thresholds could signal improving buying momentum and offer some relief to investors after the recent losses.

Sharing a personal assessment, EGRAG Crypto said the ongoing price action appears more consistent with market manipulation designed to frighten newer investors into selling. However, the analyst raised a bigger question about whale activity, questioning whether deep-pocketed XRP holders are using the downturn to accumulate more tokens or adding to the selling pressure by offloading their holdings.

Ultimately, how whales respond to the current market weakness could offer important clues about XRP’s next direction. If large holders are absorbing the available supply, the sell-off could eventually lose momentum. Conversely, continued whale selling could make an already difficult recovery even harder for the sixth-largest cryptocurrency.

#5237. Why Is Crypto Crashing Today? Bitcoin Falls Below $73K as Zcash (ZEC) Leads Crypto Losses

Why is crypto crashing? A hot CPI report raised Fed hike bets. ZEC fell below $1,000, the worst of 20 large coins this week.

Why is crypto crashing on Wednesday, November 11? The sell-off that began with Tuesday’s hotter-than-expected U.S. inflation report has carried into today, and almost every major coin is lower.

Bitcoin (BTC) dropped below $73,000 on Tuesday, touching $72,727 on Binance, and now trades at about $73,800. Ether (ETH) briefly slipped to $2,006, while XRP and Solana (SOL) are down 2% to 3% in 24 hours. The Crypto Fear & Greed Index, a daily gauge of market mood from 0 to 100, has fallen to 29.

The report changed what traders expect from the Federal Reserve. Bond yields jumped, and bets on a larger December rate hike nearly tripled within two hours.

One coin is falling much harder than the rest. Zcash (ZEC) is a cryptocurrency launched in 2016 that lets users hide the sender, the receiver and the amount of a payment. It dropped below $1,000 on Wednesday for the first time since September 5. ZEC now trades at about $966, down 15.4% in 24 hours and 24.8% in a week.

ZEC has been one of this year’s best performers. It rose from about $526 on January 1 to a closing high of $1,653 on September 26. We checked the 20 largest coins on Binance, and ZEC fell the most over both the day and the week, even though it is still up 89% in 2026.

Trader 0x8742 opened a 10x long on 18,000 $ZEC ($17.63M) and is now down over $2.8M.

— Lookonchain (@lookonchain) November 11, 2026

So why are the markets spooked, and why is ZEC taking the hardest hit?

Inflation came in hot. The consumer price index (CPI) rose 0.4% in October, above the 0.3% economists expected. Core prices, which leave out food and energy, rose 0.3% against a 0.2% forecast.

That reading crossed a line the Fed had drawn. Fed Governor Christopher Waller said in a speech hours before the report that core inflation of about 0.3% would make a half-point hike in December worth considering.

Traders took him at his word. On Polymarket, the odds of a hike of 0.5 percentage point or more in December went from 4.4% just before the report to 12.8% two hours later. They sit at 9.4% now, while a standard quarter-point hike is priced at 85.5%.

Higher rates hurt crypto in a simple way. They make cash and bonds pay more, so money flows out of riskier bets. On Tuesday, the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield rose from 4.86% to 4.98%.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best Low-Cap Crypto

ZEC had the most to give back. We compared the price changes of 20 large coins on Binance over the past week and since January 1.

When markets get nervous, traders tend to sell what has made them the most money. ZEC has fallen 41% from its September 26 closing high of $1,653.

Borrowed money made the drop faster. On Hyperliquid, ZEC fell from $1,199.20 to $965.79 in less than a day. Lookonchain counted 32 traders holding more than $1 million each in leveraged bets on a rising ZEC price. Of those, 27 are now losing money, with about $23.7 million in paper losses between them.

Sharp drops have not always lasted. Since January 2025, ZEC has fallen 12% or more in a single day 14 times on Binance. Seven days later, it was higher eight times, with a median gain of 6.5%.

ZEC trades at about $966. It touched $961.07 on Wednesday, its lowest price since September 4.

On the daily chart, where each candle covers one day, ZEC has fallen below its 50-day exponential moving average (EMA), an average that gives recent prices more weight, at $1,200.68. It is still above its 200-day EMA at $872.23.

For Bitcoin, the line to watch is Tuesday’s low of $72,727. Below that, $68,902, the August 20 low, is the next support.

So, why is crypto crashing? A hot inflation report raised the odds of a bigger Fed hike, and ZEC’s leveraged bulls are paying the most for it. A daily close back above $1,050 would be the first sign the selling is easing.

#5236. Bitcoin’s daily volume falls to 126K BTC, lowest in 10 years: Will BTC plummet to $60K?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Amid ongoing volatility, can Bitcoin bulls prevent a pullback toward $60k?

The most recent CPI release has caused a strong risk-off move and pushed BTC down below $75k. While it is still only 4% from November open, the risk of further downside is increasing.

Technically, $75k is an important level, as it is a cost basis for short-term holders, meaning it could form a significant supply zone, with investors looking to sell at close to breakeven.

The recent Glassnode report adds another layer. As can be seen in the chart below, the largest Bitcoin liquidity cluster is concentrated around $60k. These are mostly longs opened at the bottom of the market, which haven’t been re-tested by BTC since it rallied from around $65k in August.

$60k is therefore a major downside liquidity cluster that is around 20% away from $75k.

The key takeaway? According to Glassnode analysts, a rally to $60k could result in large amounts of long-term liquidation. However, this can create an accumulation opportunity for patient accumulators, those who are looking to take advantage of the forced selling.

This certainly adds pressure at the $75k level. Technically, Bitcoin is still inside this range, and there isn’t much to lose here for bulls. But with how the recent CPI data has gone, it might be harder to stay above $75k.

The expectations for December rate cuts have changed, and the on-chain fundamentals are very bearish.

So, if there is not enough buy-side liquidity to absorb the sell-side liquidity around $75k, BTC could face a deeper correction as long liquidations accelerate. This could bring the $60k liquidity pocket into focus, where even bullish investors may find a better accumulation opportunity.

So, is Bitcoin [BTC] heading for a deeper retracement before its next recovery?

The broader liquidity crunch is now weighing on the Bitcoin network.

The stablecoin market has seen its quickest drop in supply since 2022, losing a total of $14.5 billion in 30 days. According to Glassnode, this is due to holders swapping their stablecoins back into dollars and taking funds out of crypto.

Tether alone accounted for $8.4 billion of the decline, more than half the total drop.

The result? As can be seen in the chart below, Bitcoin’s daily trading volume has dropped to a record low of 126k BTC in 2026. This is down from around 453k BTC in 2022. So, technically, there has been an overall decline of around 72% in Bitcoin’s spot trading volume.

The key takeaway? Glassnode says a return to supply growth would be the first sign that new money is coming in.

In essence, the decline in stablecoin and Spot volumes shows that there is more liquidity being drained from the market. This will leave Bitcoin much more vulnerable and will make it harder to hold the $75k level. In turn, the $60k region is looking like an area of accumulation, reinforcing Glassnode’s thesis.

Bitcoin risks a deeper drop toward $60k as selling pressure builds around $75k.

Stablecoin supply and BTC trading volume are falling, signaling less money flowing into the crypto market.

Bitcoin is trading at $73,535, down 16% from its September peak. Unfortunately, the market is brutal right now and clearly shows a serious level of pressure.

The Anthropic IPO, growing demand for memory chips, and the rapid improvement of AI push investors away from the cryptocurrency industry, which has been in pain for the last two years, lacking catalysts, safety and use cases.

#5234. USDC Enters Dual EU Supervision as EBA Oversight Takes Effect

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: USDC’s European supervision changes on November 12, with the EBA and France’s ACPR sharing responsibilities.

USDC’s European regulatory status moves from an announced designation to an operating supervisory arrangement on November 12. That is the date set in the European Banking Authority’s October decision, which classified Circle’s dollar token as the EU’s first significant e-money token under MiCA.

NewsBTC covered the original significant-token designation last month. The development now is the scheduled transfer of specified responsibilities, making this a practical test of how the EU supervises a widely used stablecoin across agencies.

The EBA takes direct responsibility for own funds, reserves, liquidity requirements, and recovery and redemption plans. France’s Autorité de Contrôle Prudentiel et de Résolution remains responsible for areas including governance, outsourcing, conflicts of interest and assessments of key personnel.

Circle Internet Financial Europe SAS is the relevant European issuing entity. The arrangement therefore divides duties between supervisors rather than replacing French oversight altogether. For anyone assessing a stablecoin provider, identifying the responsible legal entity remains part of understanding the regulatory framework.

The EBA’s explanation of its MiCA role says significant tokens face additional requirements because of the greater risks they may pose. Its framework combines assessments of significance, direct supervisory tasks and cooperation with national authorities.

For significant e-money tokens issued by electronic money institutions, the EBA focuses mainly on the additional liquidity and own-funds requirements. It also establishes and chairs a supervisory college to support cooperation and information sharing. The framework includes ongoing supervision away from the issuer’s premises alongside targeted on-site work.

Those functions address a different question from whether a token traded near one dollar yesterday. Reserves must support redemptions, and operational arrangements must hold up when conditions become less comfortable. A stable market price provides useful information, but it cannot show every weakness in an issuer’s processes.

An earlier EBA procedural decision describes powers that include requesting information, investigating, inspecting premises and taking supervisory measures. It also sets out how responsibility moves between authorities and how supervisory colleges are established.

That makes the milestone more substantial than an extra label. The significance of the transfer will emerge through continuing oversight and the issuer’s compliance with the applicable obligations. It is not a separate promise that every application using USDC will be secure or that every intermediary will process withdrawals without delay.

Consider a business receiving USDC through a payment processor. The issuer’s reserves, the processor’s handling of the transfer and the business’s own wallet controls are different parts of the transaction. Strengthening one part does not automatically validate the others.

Stablecoins are increasingly being considered within existing payment systems, including the Circle and ACI settlement work covered by NewsBTC. For a prospective user, a clear supervisory structure can make questions about accountability easier to direct.

The useful next questions concern how supervisory coordination works in practice, how the issuer explains its obligations and what public information becomes available about compliance. Businesses still need to assess their own service providers, contractual rights and operational arrangements.

November 12 establishes a defined oversight structure for a significant European stablecoin. Whether that structure delivers confidence over time will depend on its operation, especially when markets or payment systems come under pressure.

written by the News Desk and edited by Samuel Rae.

#5233. Kevin O'Leary Reveals What's Really Behind Crypto Market Crash

Renowned investor and Shark Tank personality Kevin O’Leary has shared his thoughts on the current cryptocurrency crash, stating that the crypto industry faces issues that extend far beyond Bitcoin's dropping price.

In particular, Mr. Wonderful, as O'Leary is better known, believes that one of the biggest issues the crypto industry faces is the lack of regulatory clarity in the United States. This prevents big financial firms from adopting blockchain and stablecoin technologies.

According to O'Leary, institutional crypto adoption has slowed to a crawl. Companies are unwilling to invest any capital until there is clarity around regulations.

"It's not just about Bitcoin in the crypto sell-off. The adoption of stablecoins and blockchain within institutions has slowed down. Companies are waiting for clarity on regulations," O'Leary wrote.

The billionaire investor shared that his company already utilizes Circle's USDC for treasury management and international settlements. However, many other companies are afraid to follow suit since their compliance teams aren't sure about the regulatory landscape.

"We use USDC for treasury management and cross-border settlement. But many others won't do it till their compliance guys know the rules," O'Leary added.

The famous investor focused on two major U.S. bills regarding cryptocurrency: the GENIUS Act and the CLARITY Act.

"We got the GENIUS Act going. We need the CLARITY Act for the rest," Kevin O'Leary concluded.

O'Leary has long raised concerns about the crypto space and the reasons why Bitcoin is not seeing more institutional investment yet.

For example, on Oct. 28, the billionaire investor named two main issues affecting Bitcoin. The first was regulatory uncertainty, and the second was the rising threat of quantum computing.

#5232. Bank of America Warns of 50 bps Fed Hike in December: Is Bitcoin Heading for Another 2022-Style Drop?

Bank of America (BofA) says hot CPI puts a 50bps December Fed hike on the table. BTC fell a week later after 5 of the last 6 big hikes.

Bank of America said this week that the Federal Reserve could raise interest rates by half a percentage point in December. The warning came after Tuesday’s October inflation report ran hotter than expected for a third month in a row. In Bitcoin news, the coin trades at $73,888 on Binance, down 0.6% in 24 hours. It dropped to $72,727 on Tuesday after the report, its lowest price since August 20.

The bank still expects the Fed to raise rates by 0.25 percentage points, or 25 basis points (bps), at its December 8–9 meeting. But it says the case for a bigger move is growing, and it calls the Fed’s decision to hold rates in October a possible policy mistake.

Traders are starting to listen. On Polymarket, the odds of a hike of 50 bps or more in December rose from 0.6% on Monday to 11.4% on Wednesday.

BANK OF AMERICA WARNS FED COULD DELIVER 50BPS HIKE IN DECEMBER

Bank of America says October's hot CPI report virtually guarantees a December Fed rate hike — and opens the door to a larger 50bps move.

Headline CPI rose 0.4%, while core CPI climbed 0.3%, marking a third…

— *Walter Bloomberg (@DeItaone) November 11, 2026

The bank’s call rests on three months of hot data. Consumer prices rose 0.4% in October, and core prices, which leave out food and energy because they swing the most, rose 0.3%. Both beat forecasts, the third upside surprise in a row in the Bureau of Labor Statistics report.

Bank of America now estimates that core PCE inflation, the Fed’s preferred measure, is running at 3.3% a year. The Fed’s target is 2%. The bank says price rises are spreading from goods to services, with electronics, airfares and hotels leading the increases.

Its base case is still a 25 bps hike in December. The change is in the risks: a 50 bps move is now possible, and the bank sees a growing chance of another hike in January instead of March.

The bank is not alone. Fed Governor Christopher Waller said in a speech on Tuesday morning that he leans toward a 25 bps hike in December. If core inflation came in around 0.3% again, he said, a 50 bps hike “should be considered.” It did.

Higher rates make cash and bonds pay more. That tends to pull money out of risky assets like BTC, and a faster pace of hikes pulls harder.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best New Crypto to Hodl

The size of the hike has mattered for BTC. We measured Bitcoin’s price on Binance from the 2 p.m. ET announcement of each Fed rate hike since 2022 to one week later. That covers 12 hikes: six of 50 bps or more and six of 25 bps.

After the six big hikes, BTC was lower a week later five times. The median move was a 6% drop. The worst came after the 50 bps hike in May 2022, when BTC fell 23% in a week.

After the six 25 bps hikes, the picture was milder. BTC was lower a week later four times, but the median move was a drop of just 0.6%.

The record comes with a caveat. All six big hikes happened in 2022, a bear market that also saw the collapses of Terra in May and FTX in November. Those crashes made the falls after the May and November hikes worse.

The most recent hike was a 25 bps move on September 16. BTC rose 11.3% in the week after it, as the uncertainty that had built up before the meeting cleared. That makes the size of December’s hike, more than the hike itself, the Bitcoin news to watch.

#5231. Laser Digital just won Japan’s first crypto license in 4 years – Nomura might sell it

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Nomura Holdings, one of Japan’s largest financial services companies, is reportedly considering selling its cryptocurrency subsidiary, Laser Digital, amid continued financial losses in its European operations.

According to Bloomberg, Nomura has held discussions with potential buyers, including financial services firm Marex Group. However, the company has not made a final decision yet and could still choose to retain the business.

This followed Nomura’s European operations reporting a pretax loss of 7.4 billion yen ($46.5 million) in the third quarter of 2026, marking their fourth consecutive quarterly loss. However, it’s not yet confirmed by Nomura whether Laser Digital was responsible for any part of the financial setback.

For background, Laser Digital was established in 2022 and provides institutional crypto trading, asset management, and investment services. Despite the possibility of a sale, the subsidiary has continued expanding its operations.

In fact, in August 2026, Laser Digital Japan secured the country’s first new crypto exchange registration in approximately four years, allowing it to offer institutional trading and liquidity services.

It also received preliminary approval from the US Office of the Comptroller of the Currency in May to establish a national trust bank.

But it looks like market volatility and financial pressures may have prompted the company to reconsider its investment.

This is because the global crypto market’s trading volume has fallen to $64.513B after a drop of 21.3% in the past 24 hours. Meanwhile, the Crypto Fear and Greed Index also stood at 28 in the ‘Fear’ zone at press time.

At the same time, Japan’s latest inflation data has increased expectations that the Bank of Japan (BOJ) could raise interest rates again in December, potentially creating volatility across global financial markets.

This prediction comes as the producer prices rose 7.1% in October, exceeding economists’ expectations of 6.5%.

Import prices also surged 24.8% in yen terms, largely because the weak Japanese currency has made imported fuel, raw materials and other goods more expensive.

That said, another hike in December would mark the first time since 1989 that the country has raised interest rates three times in one calendar year.

This followed the United States’ reportedly joining Japan in supporting the yen through a coordinated foreign exchange intervention, marking its first such involvement since 1998.

Needless to say, the potential rate increase could also affect Bitcoin [BTC] and US stocks through the yen carry trade. Now, though, Japan has an estimated 5 million cryptocurrency owners, but its crypto adoption remains relatively limited.

According to market estimates, Japan’s cryptocurrency market was valued at $1.69 billion in 2025 and is expected to grow to $1.98 billion in 2026. It could reach $7.12 billion by 2034, representing a compound annual growth rate (CAGR) of 17.32% between 2026 and 2034.

This coincided with a recent report by AMBCrypto, wherein Japan’s major banking groups, including MUFG, SMBC, and Mizuho, are exploring yen stablecoins and cross-border payments.

#5230. Russia Sets November 22 Start for Exchange Crypto Trading, Subject to Approval

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Russian exchanges may begin cryptocurrency trading from November 22 if their trading rules receive the Bank of Russia’s concurrence. The November 11 announcement sets operating requirements for venues. A separate asset-eligibility ordinance takes effect on November 17, so the two dates describe different regulatory steps.

Russia has added another piece to its regulated crypto market framework, with a new ordinance setting the conditions under which exchanges can start trading digital currencies and digital rights. The Bank of Russia’s November 11 notice identifies November 22 as the earliest start under those rules, subject to the regulator agreeing to a venue’s trading arrangements.

That condition matters. An effective date creates a legal opening; it does not show that every exchange is technically ready or that every prospective customer can place an order on the same morning.

Under the announcement, exchanges must register trading participants, maintain records of orders and transactions, and monitor trading. They also have to calculate specified prices and report trading information to the central bank using the procedures applied to other assets.

These requirements address the organisation of a market: who participates, how activity is recorded and what the supervisor can inspect. The notice says the Ministry of Justice has registered the ordinance. It does not name a completed launch at a particular exchange or provide a forecast for trading volumes.

The central bank’s November 6 asset-eligibility announcement set a November 17 effective date for an ordinance covering Bitcoin, Ether and USDT. That notice deals with which assets non-qualified investors may access and the conditions attached to their purchases.

Non-qualified investors face an annual purchase ceiling of 300,000 rubles through a single intermediary. Qualified investors are not subject to that asset-list and purchase-limit restriction. The central bank says investors in both groups must pass the relevant test and familiarise themselves with crypto investment risks before transacting.

Reading the announcements together avoids a misleading single-date headline. One concerns the eligible assets and investor conditions; the other concerns organised trading requirements. Neither removes the need to check when a particular intermediary actually makes a service available.

A national regulatory opening can exist alongside changes at individual international exchanges. NewsBTC previously reported KuCoin’s withdrawal from Russia and Belarus, where affected users’ account notices determine their closure deadlines.

Those developments should be assessed separately. A domestic venue’s permission does not change another company’s service terms, and an international exchange exit does not by itself tell us when a domestic alternative will be usable.

For a trader, the practical questions include the identity of the intermediary, the assets it supports, how a purchase is funded and where holdings are kept. For an exchange, reliable records and price calculations become part of delivering a functioning service rather than merely announcing one.

The market impact depends on participation after services open. An expanded route into Bitcoin could bring additional buyers, but it could also shift activity from one channel to another. Permission alone cannot distinguish those outcomes or establish how much fresh capital will arrive.

There is a longer institutional backdrop. NewsBTC reported VTB’s conditional plans for direct crypto trading in December 2025. That historical proposal helps explain interest in domestic access, but it is not evidence that the bank has launched a service under the latest rules.

The next meaningful evidence from Russia will be venue-specific launch notices, completed onboarding and observable trading activity. Until those arrive, November 22 is best understood as a conditional regulatory starting point. It is a material step toward an organised market, with implementation still determining what customers can actually do.

Anthropic’s highly anticipated IPO, ticker ANT, is set to price in a $70–$78 range and begin trading on Nasdaq around mid-to-late November 2026, with a targeted valuation as high as $1.4 trillion. However, in a wild turn of events, Anthropic’s own Claude AI predicts that its own share price could tank upon launch.

Yet in an over-the-top bearish scenario, the stock could suffer one of the most dramatic post-IPO collapses in modern market history by January 1, 2027. The landmark catalyst that unlocks this downside is a highly unlikely but not impossible convergence of events: a high-profile safety incident or regulatory action involving Claude models coincides with the deepening fallout from the Nvidia CEO scandal and a broader political backlash against frontier AI.

In this hypothetical, revelations of serious alignment failures or misuse cases trigger emergency congressional hearings, restrictive new rules from multiple governments, and a sudden freeze in enterprise adoption. Combined with already fragile sentiment around massive AI infrastructure spending, the narrative flips from “indispensable technology” to “overhyped liability,” prompting a rapid and severe derating of the entire sector.

(SOURCE: Claude AI Predicts a Disastrous Anthropic IPO)

Because ANT has not yet begun trading, pure technical analysis is necessarily forward-looking. In the bearish path, the stock would open with an initial pop or modest premium but quickly form a series of lower highs as institutional sellers and lock-up-related supply emerge.

A failure to hold the IPO price range as support, followed by a break below key psychological levels such as $60 and then $50, would confirm a distribution pattern. Volume spikes on down days, deteriorating relative strength versus the Nasdaq, and a rapid slide into oversold RSI territory would support the case for further downside, especially if the broader AI complex continues to de-rate.

#5227. Bottomline Completes First USDC Transfers Inside Its Enterprise Payments Platform

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Bottomline has completed live USDC transfers inside its enterprise payments platform. The initial transfer was between company entities.

Bottomline disclosed the cross-border transactions in a November 10 announcement, using its Global Payments Hub and Cash Management platform.

The practical point is where the payment fits. A finance team may be more interested in approving, tracking and reconciling a transfer within its existing systems than in opening a separate crypto application to make it happen.

Bottomline describes the milestone as an intra-company transfer. That establishes that the company used the integration for a live movement of funds; it does not establish that unrelated businesses are already using it at scale.

The release says the aim is to let treasury teams handle stablecoin payments alongside traditional methods, retaining familiar approvals, controls and audit processes. It presents near-real-time settlement for selected scenarios, especially cross-border payments, as part of the attraction.

The company has not supplied a transaction-by-transaction performance dataset in this announcement. Readers therefore cannot use it to calculate an average fee saving or a reliable improvement in end-to-end delivery time.

Bottomline’s cash-management product description emphasises connecting treasury, accounting, compliance, banking and enterprise resource planning data. It describes tools for cash visibility, reconciliation and reporting, helping place the USDC announcement within the wider product.

Consider a group with businesses in two countries. Moving value between them is only one part of the job. Staff may also need to identify the correct entities, record the purpose of the transfer, obtain approval and match the completed payment to the accounts.

If a stablecoin route requires those steps to be reconstructed manually outside the normal workflow, a fast blockchain transfer can still leave an expensive administrative task. Integration is useful when it keeps the financial record attached to the movement of money.

NewsBTC explored a related implementation issue in Circle and ACI’s Latin American settlement collaboration, which examined how a digital-dollar option fits within existing payment operations.

Circle describes USDC as a dollar-redeemable token backed by liquid reserves. That explains the asset being transferred, but a corporate payment can involve more than the onchain movement itself.

A business funding a transfer from a bank account needs an entry route. A recipient that pays suppliers in local currency may need conversion and a bank payout. Each stage can affect the amount received and the time before it can be spent.

For that reason, the useful comparison is between two complete payment routes serving the same need. A low network charge does not settle the comparison if conversion costs are higher, or if an exception requires substantial staff time to resolve.

The next useful disclosures would identify supported use cases, the businesses choosing them and performance over repeated transfers. Clear handling of failures, rejected payments and reconciliation exceptions would be particularly informative.

There is also a difference between a first transaction and a mature operating metric, as NewsBTC’s analysis of Bitso Business payment volumes explains. Neither measure should be mistaken for fee revenue or universal customer availability.

Bottomline has demonstrated a concrete integration step. Its commercial value will come from making a recurring treasury task easier to complete and account for, with enough transparency for finance teams to compare the new route with the options they already trust.

#5224. Ripple’s Bank-Style Lending on XRPL Nears Launch: What XRP Holders Actually Get

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

The LendingProtocolV1_1 amendment on the XRP Ledger (XRPL) has collected 22 of 35 votes (62.86%).

Three more validators have signed on over the past three weeks. Ripple's own nodes have already voted yes, along with the University of Waterloo and UC Berkeley.

To go live, the amendment has to clear the 80% bar (29 votes) and hold it for two straight weeks.

The related SingleAssetVault amendment (XLS-65) has 23 votes (65.7%) and needs six more.

The XLS-65 and XLS-66 standards drop overcollateralization and instant liquidations and let companies take out fixed-term, uncollateralized loans directly on XRPL. The loans work similarly to traditional bank credit, with a fixed term and pre-set interest terms.

Ripple has already tested the protocol on a private XRPL network with nine nodes that matched the hardware of its mainnet nodes.

Holders deposit XRP or stablecoins such as RLUSD into Single Asset Vaults and receive shares. Interest is paid on a cash basis. Depositors receive it only after the borrower has sent the money. Withdrawals from a vault are processed on a first-come, first-served basis.

Borrowers are vetted off-chain by Loan Brokers, who record each disbursement on the ledger. According to the XRPL documentation, the protocol "relies on off-chain underwriting and risk management to assess the creditworthiness of borrowers."

The broker's own "first-loss capital" covers losses first when a client stops paying.

Issuers can also freeze assets or claw them back. According to Ripple, all operations in a vault and in the connected lending protocol stop when an asset issuer freezes the vault.

Until now, XRP holders who wanted passive income had to use third-party bridges and wrapped tokens. After the amendment activates, holders can deposit XRP in a pool on XRPL, with its fraction-of-a-cent fees, and collect part of the interest paid by borrowers. Their income will depend on the terms set by the loan broker and on borrowers' repayments.

If a major borrower defaults and the broker's first-loss capital runs out, depositors bear the losses and their shares drop in value.
