#5223. How will Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang’s resignation affect crypto investors like you?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

When it rains, it pours. That’s how Nvidia investors feel right now.

The financial market had painstakingly priced in news of Nvidia’s President and CEO having an affair with a Chinese spy. Over the last 24 hours, however, it has been revealed that he also had a relationship with a junior executive at the company itself.

Simply put, sh*t hit the fan. And, NVDA’s performance highlighted that reality. At the time of writing, NVDA was trading at $164.23, down 14% in the last 5 days alone.

Thus, it was no surprise that Jensen Huang resigned. In a statement, the company’s board announced that CFO Colette Kress will take over as Interim CEO until a more permanent hire is made. The statement added,

Jensen’s contributions to NVIDIA are profound. NVIDIA is one of the most consequential technology companies in the world. The Board and the company thank Jensen for his immeasurable contributions to the company and the industry.

Now that the resignation is past us, it’s worth taking a stock of the situation. To be specific, what does his resignation mean for the crypto sector?

The most immediate risk is one of spill-offs. Market analysts often see tech stocks, especially high-growth ones, and crypto as correlated risk-on assets. Thanks to the AI boom, Nvidia now accounts for 8% of the NASDAQ 100 and 7% of the S&P 500. This mean that any volatility in NVDA’s figures, significant or not, will have some impact on broader tech indices.

This impact is likely to extend to the crypto market as well, with fund managers likely to sell liquid assets like Bitcoin to raise cash or meet margin requirements if institutions see heavy losses in the tech sector.

This seemed to be the case at press time too, with the world’s largest cryptocurrency’s price hitting its lowest level on the charts in months. A hotter than expected U.S CPI weighed on the crypto market too.

That’s not all either. Publicly-traded Bitcoin miners like IREN and Core Scientific could also face new restrictions as far as their recent pivot to AI-driven HPC hosting is concerned. This is a pivot that has gathered steam over the last few years as Bitcoin mining became increasingly unprofitable, one driven by Nvidia’s GPU allocations and vendor financing.

Given what’s going on with the company, a new board with a new CEO might just play it safe and act cautiously. Especially until the leadership void is filled. This will affect not just borrowing costs for the aforementioned miners, but it could also increase underwriting risks. For instance, Christopher Brown, Senior Vice President for Investments at Synovus Securities, told Reuters,

Lenders will ask what happens to that support if Nvidia’s leadership or regulatory standing is impaired, and Nvidia’s own willingness to extend more guarantees may be constrained.

This leadership void is a major issue. Not just for the company, but for any sector or market this company will affect too. In fact, this is a concern market analysts have already shared over the past few days. This was a sentiment shared by Australian fund manager Ten Cap’s Jun Bei Lu with Reuters,

There’s ​just a lot of uncertainty to come … not just for Nvidia; it’s for the whole AI — all the businesses that they co-invest and sponsor. I think ⁠the fear is that the music might stop.

Finally, there is the matter of individual cryptos in the market. Yes, the market as a whole will be affected, but it is also pretty evident that some will be more affected than others.  Especially since outside of what has been previously mentioned, the intersection of Nvidia with the crypto sector also includes cryptos linked to decentralized computing, GPU-backed infrastructure, and AI-linked assets.

These altcoins have often reacted to Nvidia-driven announcements and updates in the past, including keynotes and financial results. The significant sell-offs among these cryptos on the back of the Jensen Huang news means that resignations are a catalyst too. Except not a bullish one.

Just look at the likes of RENDER and NEAR, for instance.

RENDER’s price fell by over 11% in the 5 days after The New York Times report about Jensen and Alice Huang’s affair. At the time of writing, the crypto was bleeding fast as news of Huang’s resignation broke out.

Similarly, NEAR’s price has fallen by close to 30% since that report was first published. In the last 24 hours alone, NEAR was down by 9%, with its freefall on the charts likely to get worse as news of the resignation goes far and wide.

In many ways, it’s a little difficult to assess what will happen to Nvidia, the AI sector, and the crypto sector. Yes, there will be some volatility, but it’s the long-term that counts. A better indication would be to see how the market prices in and reacts to Nvidia’s financial results for Q3 2026. They are set to be released on 17th November.

A little more clarity on the leadership front and the future of the company’s commitments would be useful too.

#5222. Circle and Global Citizen Plan USDC Vendor Payments for Move Afrika in 2027

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Circle and Global Citizen plan USDC vendor payments and training for Move Afrika, starting in Rwanda in 2027.

The businesses behind live concerts are the focus of Circle’s latest stablecoin partnership. A November 10 announcement with Global Citizen outlines plans to offer USDC payments to vendors working on Move Afrika, the touring initiative created by Global Citizen and pgLang.

The programme offers a practical way to judge whether digital-dollar payments improve a business process beyond the initial announcement.

The partners say the payments initiative will begin in Rwanda in 2027. Circle also plans to support financial education, technical training and guidance through the Move Afrika Production Alliance.

The combination matters because receiving a token is not the same as being comfortable managing it. For a vendor, the relevant questions may be basic: how to recognise a legitimate payment, keep access to the funds and use the money for the next business expense.

Training would be especially useful if it covers those everyday decisions in the same setting as the payment itself. The partners’ ambitions will ultimately be easier to assess through the experiences of participating businesses than through a broad estimate of the market available to stablecoins.

Imagine a local production supplier that needs to pay staff and replace equipment after an event. A faster incoming transfer could be valuable, but only if the supplier can turn that balance into the payments its business needs to make.

A digital-dollar balance may be convenient for one expense and require conversion for another. Availability, fees and the reliability of the withdrawal route can therefore matter as much as the speed of the original transfer. Those are implementation questions for this programme, rather than assumptions that a dollar token resolves automatically.

The same distinction appears in NewsBTC’s coverage of SAT Japan’s stablecoin payment option: customer payment choice and the currency a business ultimately uses can be separate parts of a service.

The partnership follows Circle’s March collaboration with Sasai Fintech, a Cassava Technologies business. That earlier announcement focused on exploring USDC applications across enterprise payments, cross-border transfers and wallet services.

The two efforts have different routes to users. Sasai brings an existing financial-services platform, while Move Afrika supplies an event-based network of working businesses. Neither announcement establishes the results of the other, and the earlier collaboration should not be presented as evidence that the new vendor programme is already operating.

For Circle, the potential benefit of an event programme is the ability to connect a payment with a recognisable commercial obligation. For participating suppliers, the meaningful outcome would be a useful balance received and recorded with less friction.

The clearest future evidence would include how many vendors choose USDC, whether they use it again and the total cost of receiving and spending or converting it. Feedback about training and payment exceptions would help show where the experience needs improvement.

NewsBTC’s analysis of Bitso Business payment volumes shows why operating evidence matters. For Move Afrika, the useful evidence will come from participating vendors after the planned rollout begins.

Move Afrika gives Circle and Global Citizen an identifiable starting group and a future rollout date. The interesting story now is how that plan becomes a reliable payment experience for the people doing the work behind the stage.

#5220. Why Is Tim Cook Being Mentioned as Nvidia’s Next CEO?

Home » Crypto Market News » Why Is Tim Cook Being Mentioned as Nvidia’s Next CEO?

As prediction markets price in a Jensen Huang exit, Yale’s Jeffrey Sonnenfeld names an outside successor.

Former Apple CEO Tim Cook has entered the conversation as a potential successor to Jensen Huang after Yale professor Jeffrey Sonnenfeld floated his name in Time magazine, citing Cook’s unique track record following a major tech founder.

Now serving as Apple’s executive chairman after stepping down in September, Cook is drawing speculative interest on prediction markets as reports surrounding Huang’s personal life and links with alleged Chinese spy spark boardroom scrutiny at the world’s largest chipmaker.

When Sonnenfeld weighed potential outside replacements alongside former Google CEO Eric Schmidt, Cook stood out for several practical reasons that mirror Nvidia’s current position:

Still, there is no indication that Nvidia has approached Cook or that he is actively seeking the job. And to understand why succession is even being discussed, it helps to look at the controversy unfolding inside Nvidia’s boardroom.

Since November 8, media reports have alleged that Huang had an affair with a woman suspected of ties to Chinese intelligence—a relationship he reportedly acknowledged in a letter to staff.

The revelations have raised immediate questions around national security risks and executive succession. In response, Nvidia’s board launched an independent investigation, sending Nvidia shares down more than 8% in after-hours trading.

While the board investigates, prediction market traders are already betting aggressively on how soon Huang could step down—and who might replace him.

On Polymarket, traders are giving an 88% chance that Huang leaves his position by December 31, with the year-end contract drawing more than $4.7 million in volume.

Bettors also place 59% odds (across $2.43 million in volume) on an announcement happening as soon as today, November 12. On Kalshi, 63% of bettors expect Huang to depart by November 15.

Trading is equally active in the successor markets, though insiders currently lead the pack.

On Kalshi current Nvidia CFO Colette Kress leads at 30%, closely followed by Ian Buck, who runs Nvidia’s hyperscale and high-performance computing business, at 29%. Tim Cook sits at 5%.

On Polymarket, Buck holds the top spot at 25%, just ahead of Kress at 22%.

Because Nvidia sits at the center of the global AI trade, sudden leadership uncertainty ripples far beyond a single stock. Alongside Nvidia’s after-hours drop, broader tech equities and AI-linked crypto assets, including Nvidia-linked crypto derivatives and tokenized stocks, have already posted losses as markets brace for what comes next.

#5218. PNC’s Coinbase Pilot Turns Incoming USDC Into Business Bank Deposits

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: PNC’s limited pilot lets eligible businesses receive Ethereum-based USDC, automatically converted into dollars. Coinbase supplies the payment infrastructure.

PNC’s new stablecoin pilot addresses a practical business question: can a company accept a digital-dollar payment without deciding to hold digital assets? In its November 9 announcement, the bank says eligible Corporate & Institutional Banking clients can receive USDC and have it converted one-for-one into US dollars.

The service initially operates on a limited pilot basis. PNC identifies Ethereum as the supported blockchain for incoming payments. That detail matters because the name of a stablecoin alone is not enough to identify a valid payment route.

The converted funds go into a designated PNC deposit account. That means the service’s commercial purpose is payment acceptance rather than encouraging the recipient to maintain a crypto treasury.

For a supplier that invoices in dollars, such a structure could let a customer use a different payment method without changing the currency in which the supplier manages its business. The operational appeal would be a completed receivable that fits its existing accounting and banking relationship.

NewsBTC has examined a comparable separation between payment method and settlement currency in SAT Japan’s stablecoin arrangement with Triple-A. The parties and implementation differ, but the basic commercial question is familiar: what usable balance does the seller receive?

In its own description of the expanded partnership, Coinbase identifies its Payment APIs as the underlying infrastructure. PNC contributes banking and treasury-management services. Coinbase also places the pilot after an earlier collaboration that gave eligible PNC Private Bank clients access to digital-asset trading.

Those are different services. A trading arrangement helps a customer acquire or sell an asset; payment acceptance helps a business complete a commercial transaction. Treating them as interchangeable would obscure the reason a corporate finance team might be interested in the latest pilot.

NewsBTC’s coverage of Circle and ACI’s settlement demonstration examines a related effort to connect USDC with established payment infrastructure. That remains a separate project, with its own implementation and availability.

Coinbase’s banking integrations extend beyond PNC. A September 28 Citi announcement described plans for institutional stablecoin acceptance through Spring by Citi, including automatic conversion into fiat and settlement by Citi.

The Citi collaboration also outlined the reverse flow: using Citi’s Virtual Account Wallet infrastructure for Coinbase Virtual Accounts, so incoming fiat could be converted into stablecoins. These opposite directions show why the complete payment journey matters more than a broad claim that a bank has adopted crypto.

That earlier announcement is context for the business model. It does not establish PNC’s pricing, eligibility or processing times, which must be assessed on their own terms.

The useful performance measures are the time until dollars are available, the full cost to sender and recipient, and the quality of payment information that reaches the business. Exception handling matters too: a transfer that fails a check or arrives with incomplete details still needs a clear resolution process.

The public PNC and Coinbase announcements do not provide a comprehensive fee schedule or an end-to-end service guarantee. The one-for-one conversion description should therefore not be expanded into a claim that every part of the payment is free.

The pilot gives eligible businesses a defined way to test stablecoin acceptance within a familiar banking relationship. Its wider significance will depend on whether customers choose it repeatedly because it makes getting paid easier.

#5217. Why Strategy’s MSCI removal could spell trouble for Bitcoin’s Q4 outlook

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

If you thought Q4 volatility was finally cooling down, you might want to think again.

And yes, the December rate decision priced in by the market is only the tip of the iceberg. Analysts have spent much of the last 24 hours assessing the MSCI report announcing its index review on the 11th of November. Naturally, the focus immediately shifted to Strategy’s MSTR index eligibility as traders began pricing in the risk of its removal from MSIC’s global indexes. Notably, this is exactly how things played out.

In its recent review, MSCI has officially confirmed that Strategy will be removed from its global indexes, effective from the 1st of December. This put MSTR among the companies set to lose their place in these benchmarks, adding another layer of uncertainty around institutional exposure and potential selling pressure.

However, to understand why this decision matters, it is important to first understand what MSCI represents.

MSCI (Morgan Stanley Capital International) creates some of the biggest stock indices in the world. Investment funds that have over $21 trillion in assets track these indices. Many invest in stocks according to these indices. If the fund tracks an MSCI index including MSTR, then the Strategy removal may cause these investment funds to decrease or sell their holdings to match the new index.

It, therefore, comes as little surprise that traders are worried that MSTR’s removal by MSCI will cause a wave of institutional selling. Moreover, given that Strategy is still the largest corporate holder of Bitcoin [BTC] (850,364 BTC on their balance sheet), the importance of this decision cannot be understated. Less institutional exposure to MSTR could cause sentiment to turn more negative towards corporate Bitcoin treasuries.

Traders are now watching whether the removal triggers a broader risk-off move across the crypto market.

Following MSCI’s decision, MSTR’s institutional ownership is becoming an important part of the story.

Some of Wall Street’s largest asset management firms also hold large stakes within the company, such as Capital International Investors, BlackRock, Morgan Stanley, Invesco, and Goldman Sachs. Based on the latest institutional filings, it can be seen that Capital International Investors is the largest institutional investor holding around 34.9 million MSTR shares. This is followed by BlackRock with 19.39 million shares.

Interestingly, institutional investment increased within Q2. Invesco increased their shareholding by around 7.21 million shares. Meanwhile, Goldman Sachs increased theirs by 4.02 million shares, Capital International Investors increased theirs by 3.46 million shares and BlackRock increased their shareholding by around 1.64 million shares. Overall, there were around 209.52 million MSTR shares owned by institutional investors at the end of Q2, an increase of 9.62% compared to the previous quarter.

However, not every major institution was accumulating.

The move by Capital Research Global Investors, who shed around 4.62 million shares, and Bank of America, who sold 2.79 million, demonstrates that it was already a slightly negative time for institutions. The primary concern therefore will be whether MSCI’s decision to exclude MSTR may speed up institutional selling.

In this context, MSCI’s latest decision could become a turning point for Bitcoin’s late Q4 rally. If the funds tracking MSCI’s indices are forced to sell their MSTR stake, it will add more downward pressure. It may also damage institutional confidence in Strategy’s “high-risk, Bitcoin-focused” investment strategy. In turn, creating further uncertainty in the crypto markets.

MSCI’s removal could force index-tracking funds to sell MSTR shares, adding further selling pressure.

Despite rising institutional holdings in Q2, the decision could hurt confidence in Strategy and other corporate Bitcoin holders.

#5216. Bitcoin Price Crashes to $72,700 as Leveraged Traders Buy the Dip: Is the Bottom In?

The crypto market crash deepens as $1B+ in longs get liquidated. Bitcoin sits mid-range at $72,969; fear at 28 isn’t a bottom yet.

Bitcoin fell below $73,000 on Thursday, November 12, extending a week-long crypto market crash in which traders who bet on a rebound were forced out of their positions again and again.

Bitcoin touched $72,735 on Binance, a hair above Tuesday’s $72,727 low and its weakest price since August 20. It trades at $72,969, down 9.4% from the November 4 close.

BREAKING: $BTC just fell to its lowest level since August.

Over $210 million worth of longs liquidated in the past 24 hours.

The dumping continues… pic.twitter.com/R7NVGivFnD

— Crypto Rover (@cryptorover) November 12, 2026

The altcoins are doing worse. Over seven days, Ethereum has lost 16.4%, Solana 14.7% and XRP 21.3%. CoinGecko values the whole crypto market at $2.42 trillion on Thursday, and Bitcoin makes up 60.5% of it.

Leverage made every drop bigger. More than $1 billion of bets were liquidated on Monday alone, almost all of them longs, the positions that profit only if prices rise. Liquidation means the exchange closes a trade by force once the trader’s collateral runs out, and those forced sales push the price down further.

So is this the flush that ends the crypto market slide, or is there more to come? The data has an answer, and it is not a comforting one.

The sell-off had no single cause. Four pieces of bad news landed one after another:

Each hit forced out more leveraged longs across the crypto market. Thursday’s slide continued the pattern on a smaller scale. We added up every forced closure in Bitcoin, Ether, Solana, XRP and Dogecoin futures on OKX, one of the largest exchanges, over the past 24 hours. Longs lost $27.6 million, against $12.3 million for shorts.

EXPLORE: Bitcoin Price Slides Toward $73K as Nvidia Plunges 9% and MSCI Drops Saylor’s Strategy

Big liquidations usually clear out the crowd. This time, the crowd came back.

In plain terms, traders keep buying every dip with borrowed money. That leaves fuel for another round of forced selling if Bitcoin drops through the next support. Bottoms tend to come when funding turns negative and open interest falls hard, a sign that leveraged buyers have given up.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Next Crypto to Hit 1 Dollar

The Crypto Fear & Greed Index, which scores market mood from 0 (extreme fear) to 100 (extreme greed), reads 28 today. Fear is usually read as a buy signal. The history says it is not enough on its own.

We checked every day since February 2018 when the index sat at 28 or lower, 943 days in all, and where Bitcoin stood later.

This year’s two bottoms came with real panic. When Bitcoin hit $60,000 on February 6, the index read 9. When it bottomed at $57,800 on July 1, it read 11. Today’s 28 is fear, but it is not capitulation, the moment sellers give up all at once.

On the weekly chart, where each candle covers one week, Bitcoin has spent 2026 in a wide range. The low is $57,800 from July 1 and the high is $87,396 from late September. The midpoint is $72,598, almost exactly where Bitcoin trades now.

The price lost its 50-week exponential moving average (EMA), an average that gives recent prices more weight, at $78,219. Its 200-week EMA sits at $69,597. Bitcoin closed below that line for 16 weeks this year, in February and March and again from June to mid-August, and both of the year’s bottoms formed during those stretches.

For the crypto market, three signs would say the bottom is close: the fear index dropping toward the teens, funding turning negative, and open interest falling as longs give up. None has shown up yet. Until they do, the 200-week EMA at $69,597 is the line that separates a pullback inside the 2026 range from a retest of the summer lows.

#5215. Dogecoin (DOGE) Wallet Pushes Quick Fix for Bitcoin (BTC) Transactions Ahead of Major Release

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Dogecoin wallet 'MyDoge' has rolled out a quick update to address Bitcoin swap and send issues affecting users of its Classic Wallet, ahead of the planned release of version 3.1.2 this weekend.

Alex, CTO of MyDoge Wallet, announced the update, explaining that the team had pushed an interim fix to resolve the reported BTC transaction problems before the broader software release.

Pushed an update to quickly address BTC swap/send issues while using "Classic Wallet" before we release the full v3.1.2 this weekend Simply close / reopen the app and you should see: 3.1.1-652.1 or 3.1.1-652.2 in the "About MyDoge" settings

According to the announcement, users do not need to manually download a separate update. Instead, they can simply close and reopen the MyDoge app to receive the latest version.

Users can verify whether the update has been applied by navigating to the "About MyDoge" section in the app settings. The expected version numbers are 3.1.1-652.1 or 3.1.1-652.2.

In Dogecoin ecosystem news, DogeOS is nearing mainnet launch, bringing an EVM-compatible app layer to DOGE that would let developers build DeFi, games and other apps on Dogecoin for the first time.

In October, DogeOS opened a public test network for Ethereum-style trading, lending and other applications using DOGE, seeking to give Dogecoin holders more ways to use their coins beyond payments and price speculation.

In a recent post on X, Dogecoin Foundation director of product Timothy Stebbing shared a Dogecoin ecosystem 2026 update.

Stebbing predicts that the next three to four years for Dogecoin are likely to be shaped more by the development and adoption of smart-contract application layers than by large, pre-emptive changes to Dogecoin’s base protocol. He cited DogeOS as an early and visible example of that direction. If it and similar efforts succeed in attracting sustained developer activity and real usage while continuing to treat Dogecoin as the settlement and value-transfer foundation, Stebbing sees the ecosystem gaining additional utility without compromising the simplicity and reliability of the Layer 1.

However, the proposed mechanism for native proof verification on Dogecoin still requires community discussion and, ultimately, activation. Thus, adoption will depend on whether the resulting applications are useful enough that people choose to use them. None of these uncertainties change the underlying architectural advantage: the Layer 1 can remain a focused means of exchange while the surrounding ecosystem gains the capacity for richer functionality.

#5214. ASA Presses Court to Halt Stock Perpetuals, Putting Retail Risk at Center of Fight

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: The American Securities Association is asking a federal court to consider retail-investor harm in the challenge to single-stock perpetuals. Its November 10 submission, publicised on November 12, supports interim relief. The CFTC has defended the products as a regulated domestic alternative to offshore trading.

The legal battle over US single-stock perpetuals is gaining another angle: what happens to ordinary investors while the court decides whether the products were properly authorised? The American Securities Association put that question at the centre of a submission supporting the Capital Markets Alliance’s challenge to the Commodity Futures Trading Commission.

ASA announced its intervention on November 12. The accompanying court-stamped documents show that its motion and proposed friend-of-the-court brief were filed on November 10, an important distinction between the announcement date and the legal event.

In its filed motion and brief, ASA says it represents a perspective different from the exchange and trading firms arguing about competitive injury. It focuses on retail investors and the wider equity market.

The association contends that leverage, forced liquidation and trading during thinner sessions could expose customers to substantial losses and transmit stress into underlying shares. Those are arguments advanced by an interested participant, not findings that the predicted harm has occurred.

ASA is asking for a halt while the dispute proceeds. Its submission itself neither grants that relief nor settles the legal classification of perpetual contracts.

The regulator’s position is set out in Chairman Michael Selig’s November 3 statement. The CFTC says the pathway involves designated contract markets that are also notice-registered with the SEC for security-futures products.

Selig argues that bringing perpetual contracts into regulated US markets gives participants a domestic route to instruments used for risk management and price discovery. He also describes cooperation between the CFTC and SEC as central to the framework.

That position and ASA’s objections address different sides of the same policy trade-off. Supporters emphasise regulated availability; opponents dispute whether the chosen legal category preserves the protections the law requires. Describing the debate simply as regulation versus no regulation misses that disagreement.

NewsBTC previously covered the crypto and trading firms supporting the CFTC. ASA’s intervention adds an opposing retail-investor argument to that wider dispute.

In the November 3 temporary-restraining-order decision, Judge Valerie Caproni denied emergency relief because the plaintiff had not demonstrated the required imminent, irreparable harm. The judge nevertheless considered the plaintiff likely to succeed on its statutory-authority argument at that stage.

The result was therefore narrower than a final endorsement of the approvals. ASA’s emphasis on retail harm is relevant to the interim-relief question, but the court must determine what weight that submission deserves.

An example appears in the SEC’s September notice of Nadex contract specifications: the proposed perpetual products have no fixed expiry, are cash-settled and do not convey ownership of the underlying security. That document describes a particular venue’s filing rather than every contract involved in the lawsuit.

NewsBTC’s coverage of Kalshi’s separate security-futures listing standards also shows why individual filings need to be read carefully. Similar product names do not make all terms or regulatory questions identical.

The next decisive development is judicial treatment of the requested relief. Another party’s argument can sharpen the dispute, but only the court’s orders establish what the litigation changes for the affected products. Any restrictions would depend on the terms and effective timing of a court order, which an advocacy announcement cannot establish.

#5213. EU Assembles Task Force To Counter Russia

Home » Regulations » EU Assembles Task Force To Counter Russia

The EU announced two initiatives to bolster the bloc’s sanctions toolbox with enhanced global presence.

The European Union’s (EU) fresh crypto-focused task force is set to tackle top sanction evading challenges, mostly ascribed to Russia. Commissioner Maria Luís Albuquerque announced this to the public following the tenth meeting of the High-Level Expert Group on Union Restrictive Measures.

After the gathering in Brussels, this new EU regulatory arm aims to close gaps that allow Russia to sustain economic activity amid ongoing sanctions linked to its Ukraine invasion. The meeting brought together senior representatives from EU countries, the European Commission, and the External Action Service.

INTEL: EU sets up crypto task force to stop Russia dodging sanctions with digital assets

The examination of emerging financial threats revolves around crypto currency as a safe haven for tax or sanction evasion, keeping a strong commitment to Ukraine. Complementing the task force represents a broader dedicated sanctions office within the EU Commission. This improves coordination across member states.

The G7 is taking a more unified stance across policy, outreach & implementantion. It builds on 21 successive EU sanctions packages on Russia that have gradually targeted crypto-powered channels. Over the next 12 months, intelligence sharing and support from Ukraine and the UK will be used  to identify regular circumvention methods.

The latest sanction package, penned last Summer, freshly imposed transaction bans on 14 third country crypto platforms, enabling potential full bans on crypto services as well. By focusing on detection, enforcement consistency & international cooperation, the EU seeks to limit Russia’s ability to maintain economic resilience, still prevalent even after a flurry of sanction packages.

EU Launches Crypto Task Force to Crack Down on RussiaThe European Union has established a dedicated Crypto Sanctions Task Force to tackle sanctions evasion through digital assets.Announced on November 6, the program seeks to address the means through which Russia can evade… pic.twitter.com/uYJkWjr0Ez

Meanwhile, Russia recently implemented a new digital-asset focused law, lifting transaction & investment bans for retail customers that pass a special knowledgeability test. For businesses, the landscape has shifted too: Russian currency exchanges are able to legally offer crypto services from November 22, 2026, abiding by the legal framework from the local bank authority.

With the geopolitical situation evolving, cross-border transactions powered by the blockchain is seen as a seamless way to lift sanctions & off-day barriers, but it also facilitates a gray area of liquidity for military-industrial circumvention, off-record energy exports & often shadow fleet operations. To mitigate the risks, the EU’s new task force launched new enforcement measures.

#5212. What's Next For Bitcoin (BTC)? CME FedWatch Shows 100% Odds of a December Fed Rate Hike

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

CME FedWatch puts the implied chance of a Fed rate hike at 100% for the December 9 meeting, according to the November 12 snapshot. This is another source of pressure on Bitcoin, and there is a good chance the market is going to face its full effect.

Bitcoin and the cryptocurrency market in general aren't set in stone and are highly affected by the performance of traditional markets and the global economy. A rate hike directly affects the desire of investors to expose themselves to riskier assets like crypto.

Bitcoin has been going down rapidly even without the additional source of pressure from the Fed. The current BTC price drop appears to be mostly fueled by insufficient compensation for the risk: Bitcoin and crypto in general aren't as attractive as tech or AI stocks, so it is far better to get exposed to the latter ones.

That is the bearish reading here. FedWatch reflects futures pricing, so the hike is already expected rather than guaranteed to become a fresh shock.

Bitcoin is trading at $72,744, below all the moving averages shown on the daily chart. A drop below $70,000 shouldn't necessarily trigger any substantial surge of selling pressure, considering how stretched the decline already looks.

The imbalance of inflows and outflows is still staggering in this reading of the market. The concern is that almost every top-15 crypto asset remains in endurance mode, facing persistent outflows fueled by security concerns, a retreat in investor sentiment and a lack of substantial recovery prospects.

This view draws on Coinglass, CryptoQuant and SosoValue, which track derivatives, on-chain activity and ETF behavior respectively. The interpretation is that all three fields show outflows from digital assets alongside a growing supply of stablecoins.

A full-blown recovery of the cryptocurrency market would need new catalysts and use cases. A potential burst of the AI bubble, with substantial losses for the largest market players, might push them towards digital assets. A transition is possible, but it's not guaranteed by any means.

The confirmation of a market recovery would only come if we witness higher highs and constant inflows into institutional products like ETFs.

#5211. Jensen Huang Resigns as Nvidia CEO After Second Affair Bombshell: Who Is Colette Kress?

Following Huang’s resignation as Nvidia CEO, who is the Apple exec being tipped for the permanent role, and how has the NVDA price reacted?

Today (November 12, 2026), Nvidia announced that co-founder and longtime CEO Jensen Huang has resigned as President, Chief Executive Officer, and Board member, effective immediately.

The decision follows intense scrutiny over personal and potential national-security-related controversies. Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress has been appointed Interim CEO while remaining CFO. https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aioLZFt2Xwc The board has also named longtime director Harvey C. Jones as its first-ever Chair and launched a search for a permanent successor, with a high-profile Apple exec tipped as an early frontrunner.

Nvidia shares reacted sharply, falling more than -10% in premarket trading and extending recent losses that have erased hundreds of billions in market value. As of midday trading, NVDA was fluctuating near the mid-$150s to low-$160s range.

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang allegedly has a second affair on top of the suspected Chinese spy.He also had a romantic relationship with Kimberly Powell, someone who worked at Nvidia since 2008, according to a New York Times report. pic.twitter.com/QJRTnJb8c6— Pubity (@pubity) November 11, 2026

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang allegedly has a second affair on top of the suspected Chinese spy.He also had a romantic relationship with Kimberly Powell, someone who worked at Nvidia since 2008, according to a New York Times report. pic.twitter.com/QJRTnJb8c6

The resignation stems from revelations of two extramarital affairs. The first involved Alice Huang, a businesswoman linked to the Singapore-based AI company Megaspeed, a significant Nvidia customer. US authorities have identified her as a suspected asset of China’s Ministry of State Security.

Reports indicated Huang had been photographed with her on multiple occasions, raising concerns about potential intelligence targeting of the Nvidia CEO.

Huang publicly apologized to employees for being unfaithful to his wife but denied compromising national security or company secrets. The situation escalated further when additional reporting alleged a second relationship with Kimberly Powell, Nvidia’s vice president of healthcare and a direct report to Huang.

These dual revelations prompted Nvidia’s board to open an internal investigation and ultimately accept Huang’s resignation after a late-night meeting.

The combination of personal misconduct involving a subordinate and the national-security implications surrounding the alleged Chinese intelligence connection proved decisive.

https://twitter.com/JesseCohenInv/status/2120779503119012207 Colette Kress has served as Nvidia’s Executive Vice President and Chief Financial Officer since September 2013. Before joining the company, she spent nearly 25 years in senior finance roles at major technology firms,

These firms include Cisco (where she was CFO of the Business Technology and Operations organization) and Microsoft (where she was CFO of the Server and Tools division), as well as earlier positions at Texas Instruments.

She holds a bachelor’s degree in finance from the University of Arizona and an MBA from Southern Methodist University. During her tenure as CFO, Nvidia’s revenue grew dramatically from roughly $4Bn to over $200Bn as the company became the dominant force in AI accelerators.

As Interim CEO, she is tasked with providing continuity while the board searches for a permanent replacement, with Apple’s Johny Srouji tipped as an early frontrunner. Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Reveals Upcoming Coinbase Listings

Hyperliquid offers leveraged perpetual contracts tied to Nvidia’s share price, allowing traders 24/7 exposure without holding actual shares. These instruments track the underlying stock closely and have seen heavy volume during the scandal.

Since the initial reports of the affair and board investigation emerged in early November, the Hyperliquid NVDA perpetual has mirrored the traditional stock’s sharp decline.

The mark price has dropped from near or above $170–$175 to the mid-$150s, settling around $158–$160 by November 12, representing roughly a 9–11% decline in the most recent sessions alone and larger cumulative losses from pre-scandal highs.

Open interest has remained elevated (hundreds of millions of dollars), reflecting active speculative positioning, while funding rates have fluctuated as longs and shorts adjust.

Volume spiked significantly on the resignation news, consistent with forced liquidations and rapid repositioning. Technically, the contract has broken key short-term support levels, with momentum indicators deeply oversold in the near term.

Any stabilization in traditional NVDA stock would likely be reflected quickly on Hyperliquid, though the platform’s high-leverage environment amplifies both downside volatility and the potential magnitude of a bounce.

#5210. Ethlabs Calls for Ethereum Quantum Protection in 2027 as Upgrade Priorities Shift

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Ethlabs proposes quantum readiness in 2027 and plans to withdraw its quick-slots work from Hegotá. Developers still need to agree on the network's priorities.

Ethereum's next upgrade debate is becoming a question of how much engineering effort should move toward long-term security. In a November 11 proposal, Ethlabs argues for making quantum protection and resilience against advanced AI-assisted attacks the priority for 2027.

The practical question is which improvements can share an upgrade without slowing the security work. That trade-off deserves attention even from readers who never run a validator: protocol roadmaps influence the software that wallets, exchanges and applications must eventually support.

Ethlabs distinguishes basic readiness from full performance: keeping Ethereum operational with quantum-safe options is the main objective, while matching full network capacity is the more ambitious goal. Its proposed reshuffle includes withdrawing quick slots from Hegotá and reconsidering work outside that security objective.

This is a contribution to Ethereum's development process. A research group's preferred date does not establish an activation date, and an architectural direction still needs specifications, implementations and testing. The useful evidence from here will be what client teams agree to build and what they can demonstrate together.

NewsBTC's earlier coverage of Ethereum's proposed base-layer reconstruction helps explain why changes to speed, security and account design have become closely connected.

One relevant building block is EIP-8141, the frame-transaction proposal. It would let a transaction contain separate steps for validation, fee approval and execution. The design gives accounts more flexibility over how they authorise activity, including a path away from today's elliptic-curve signatures.

It also provides for key rotation, batched operations and alternative fee payment. Those features matter because a cryptographic migration is easier to manage when an account's identity can survive a change in its authorisation method.

That creates an important implementation question. An upgrade can enable a safer signature scheme, but wallet developers still have to expose it clearly, exchanges have to recognise it, and users need a recovery path that they understand. Flexibility in the protocol only becomes useful protection when the surrounding software makes it usable.

Ethereum's quantum-security overview identifies multiple areas of exposure: ordinary account signatures, validator signatures, data commitments and some proof systems. Each has a different role, so replacing one component would leave other migration work outstanding.

The same overview says present-day quantum computers cannot break Ethereum's cryptography. Preparation is being undertaken because a transition across a large network takes time, while future hardware capability is uncertain. Hash-based approaches are among the routes being researched, with different efficiency and deployment challenges from current systems.

The distinction between a future cryptographic threat and an active compromise is essential. Readers should not interpret a more ambitious research timetable as evidence that current Ethereum accounts have suddenly been broken. NewsBTC has previously examined Justin Drake's view of quantum migration as a broader redesign opportunity.

Our reading is that the proposal's significance lies in resource allocation. Every additional feature creates implementation and review work, while concentrating on security can defer improvements that other users want sooner. Neither choice is costless.

A credible programme therefore needs measurable milestones: working clients, agreement on migration behaviour and evidence about performance under the new cryptography. It also needs a clear account of which guarantees are available at each stage.

For ETH holders, those milestones will be more informative than a calendar target alone. The debate now asks developers to turn an ambitious security objective into a sequence that the wider Ethereum ecosystem can actually deliver.

#5209. USDT fell below $1 – That’s how thin crypto liquidity has gotten

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The crypto market is at a critical point with its stablecoin liquidity continuing to shrink as the year nears its end. Over the past four weeks, more than $14.20 billion in stablecoins have left the market.

Currently, the stablecoin market cap has declined below $300 billion, which is its lowest level in 14 months. That is, the cap plunged from $313 billion to $298 billion, the fastest contraction since June 2022.

Additionally, Tether’s USDT was trading below $1 at the time of press, showing that the stablecoin’s buying power was weakening too.

This contraction implies that crypto liquidity in the market is thin and fresh capital is limited to support a bullish recovery. Both Bitcoin [BTC] and Ethereum [ETH] have been averagely on a decline when gauging this month’s performance.

This suggests that both BTC and the broader altcoin market are bearish. Thus, the stablecoin supply did not necessarily go into buying and holding like in the previous years.

Meanwhile, Bitcoin’s price remained heavily influenced by liquidation-driven moves rather than organic spot demand. As per the Binance Liquidation Accumulation Index, the volatility was driven by two-way liquidations.

Between September and November, the index has visited the upper and lower bands, at 80 and 20, respectively, more than three times each. This implies that both longs and shorts are being wiped out at this juncture.

With that, the price of BTC could continue bouncing between $60K and $87K until either bears or bulls win the battle.

At the same time, Spot demand was struggling to provide consistent support.

For instance, Binance Buy/Sell Delta has declined from 0.07 to below the neutral level, affirming the weak spot demand. In fact, traders on the exchange were selling.

Additionally, U.S. Spot Bitcoin ETFs saw net outflows of over $1.60 billion over the past 20 sessions. Over the past 24 hours, around $299.90 million exited the BTC ETFs market.

Similarly, Ethereum and Solana ETFs lost $144.81 million and $5.85 million, respectively, while those of Binance Coin [BNB] and XRP remained inactive.

Therefore, if crypto liquidity and spot demand do not return, then a general market recovery would be hard to materialize. Thus, a turnback to stablecoin market cap growth would be the first sign of new money.

#5208. XRP Risks Losing $1 Amid Aggressive $2.5 Billion Shift

As XRP continues to struggle amid the broad crypto market volatility while failing in multiple attempts to recover, key on-chain metrics are pointing to increased selling pressure among traders.

While momentum remains extremely weak, latest data from crypto analytics platform, Cryptoquant, shows that sellers have dominated both XRP’s spot and futures markets, putting it at risk of losing the long-held $1 mark.

Per the data, XRP’s cumulative volume delta (CVD) across major cryptocurrency exchanges shows that aggressive selling has dominated both its spot and futures market since September 23.

As of November 12, XRP’s Perpetual CVD on Binance has fallen from -$109 million to about -$1.1 billion. It is important to note that the CVD of an asset is a key metric that measures the difference between the volume of aggressive buy orders and aggressive sell orders over time.

While the metric turns negative when more traders are aggressively selling than buying, the -991 million negative balance in the Binance perpetual CVD signals stronger selling pressure.

With the negative balance on Binance exceeding its previous level by more than 10 times, this shows that traders have placed significantly more market sell orders than buy orders, either by opening short positions or closing existing long positions.

Apart from its futures market, further data shows that sellers have also dominated XRP’s spot activity across all major crypto exchanges, including Binance.

Notably, XRP’s All CEX Estimated Spot CVD moved from -$400 million to -$2.9 billion over the same period.

While this marks a $2.5 billion increase in the imbalance favoring sellers, it indicates that XRP spot traders have also been selling XRP more aggressively rather than buying, propelling the asset for further price declines.

#5207. Xash Brings Gold Rewards to Solana Stablecoin Users

Home » Altcoins » Solana (SOL) » Xash Brings Gold Rewards to Solana Stablecoin Users

The platform aims to tackle DeFi’s reliance on short-term incentives.

Xash, a Solana-based application that rewards users with tokenized gold for stablecoin activity, has announced its public launch at Solana Breakpoint 2026 in London, scheduled for November 15–17.

The platform aims to change how users earn rewards in decentralized finance (DeFi) by offering gold instead of temporary token incentives. Users can earn rewards by lending stablecoins, providing liquidity and trading across established Solana DeFi platforms.

The launch comes as DeFi protocols continue to face a challenge: attracting users who stay after promotional rewards expire. Xash is betting that gold-backed rewards could offer a more sustainable alternative.

Many DeFi platforms use token rewards to attract deposits and trading activity. However, users often move their funds elsewhere once those incentives end, creating a cycle known as mercenary capital.

Xash wants to address this problem by rewarding ongoing stablecoin activity with tokenized gold rather than short-lived token campaigns.

The company says the approach is intended to encourage longer-term participation in DeFi while giving users an opportunity to accumulate gold.

It also aims to benefit DeFi platforms seeking sustained activity and stablecoin issuers looking to expand the use of their tokens.

“Stablecoins don’t have a supply problem. They have a usage problem. Incentive campaigns rent users for a few weeks, then they’re gone,” said Kevin Huynh, co-founder and CEO of Xash.

Xash aggregates DeFi opportunities into a single interface, evaluating available strategies daily and presenting users with options based on potential gold rewards.

Before committing funds, users can review associated costs and estimated 30-day rewards. They can then select an opportunity and authorize transactions directly through their own wallets.

As part of the launch, the company is introducing direct withdrawals of accumulated tokenized gold rewards to users’ wallets. It is also adding a curated liquidity vault built on Kamino, allowing users to deploy stablecoins through a single deposit instead of managing individual transactions.

Xash says its trading activity does not involve directional price bets or borrowed funds. However, this does not eliminate the risks associated with DeFi. Users remain exposed to the underlying protocols and market conditions, including potential losses when using the Kamino vault.

The project was founded by Kevin Huynh, who serves as CEO, alongside Vinny Lingham, co-founder of Civic and Gyft and a former general partner at Multicoin Capital; Alen Ribic, co-founder of SweepSouth; and Llew Claasen, former executive director of the Bitcoin Foundation.

Xash’s launch comes amid growing demand for tokenized real-world assets (RWAs), particularly gold-backed digital tokens.

According to the World Gold Council, the tokenized gold market has expanded fivefold since late 2024, reaching approximately $6 billion.

Trading activity has also accelerated. Tokenized gold generated more than $90 billion in spot trading volume during the first quarter of 2026 alone, surpassing the total recorded throughout 2025, according to CoinGecko research.

Xash tests whether real-asset rewards can retain DeFi users better than short-term token incentives. Its launch also reflects rising demand for tokenized gold within the Solana ecosystem.

#5206. Apex Joins Hedera Council as Fund Servicing Moves Deeper Into Tokenization

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Apex Group has joined Hedera Council. The appointment brings a fund-services specialist into network governance, while its commercial use of Hedera remains an area for exploration.

Apex Group is adding a governance role to its digital-asset work. Hedera's November 11 announcement says the financial-services provider, which services more than $3.5 trillion in assets, has joined the council overseeing the public network.

Apex plans to explore Hedera within its tokenization initiatives, including the infrastructure needed to manage assets and coordinate settlement. The assets-serviced figure describes the scale of Apex's broader business; it is not a measure of assets committed to this network.

The appointment puts a firm familiar with running financial products inside the network's decision-making structure. That experience can matter because a tokenized fund needs much more than a token-creation tool. Someone must maintain investor records, process subscriptions and redemptions, apply transfer restrictions and explain how the digital record connects to legal ownership.

Our reading is that this is the more useful way to assess the news. A governance appointment can improve the conversation about institutional requirements, but the commercial result will depend on the products and workflows that follow.

NewsBTC's report on Solana's delivery-versus-payment infrastructure explores a related operational problem: coordinating the asset and cash sides of a transaction.

The Hedera Council's governance FAQ describes a structure with up to 39 members, each holding one vote. Responsibilities include decisions on network pricing, software updates and treasury management, alongside participation in governance transactions and operation of a network node.

Membership is a governance interest. It does not give a member ownership of treasury HBAR or an entitlement to distributions from the council. The rules also say members are not required to build applications on Hedera.

Those details help separate two kinds of commitment. Apex has entered the governance structure; a particular fund deployment, transaction volume or tokenization mandate needs its own evidence. HBAR investors should be able to follow each development without having to infer a commercial contract from a council announcement.

A separate April 2025 announcement from Tokeny documented its partnership with the then-named HBAR Foundation to bring regulated asset-tokenization technology to Hedera. That work focused on ERC-3643, a token standard designed around identity and transfer controls.

Tokeny described two integration routes: institutions could use its platform or connect its APIs to existing systems. The practical significance is that an issuer can keep established operational processes while adding blockchain functions, rather than rebuilding every part of fund administration at once.

This earlier work is background to the current governance news. It shows that infrastructure development and institutional engagement have been progressing through separate steps. It does not establish that every prospective use case has reached production.

For a fund administrator, a useful tokenized product should make an existing task easier to execute, reconcile or audit. The meaningful questions are therefore concrete: which assets are supported, which investors qualify, how transfer restrictions are enforced and how exceptions are resolved.

The same implementation challenge appears in NewsBTC's coverage of Chainlink's cross-chain dividend demonstration. Moving a financial asset onto a ledger leaves the ongoing obligations attached to that asset intact.

Apex's council seat gives it a role in shaping the infrastructure. The next evidence to watch is named deployments with defined servicing arrangements and measurable activity. That will show how far governance participation translates into financial products that clients use.

written by the News Desk and edited by Samuel Rae.

#5205. Bitcoin Mining Legend Chun Wang Rebuys ETH at 14% Discount After $18.5 Million Sale

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

F2Pool co-founder Chun Wang, known on X as Satofishi, bought 5,246 ETH worth $10.78 million over the past two days at an average price of $2,054, according to Lookonchain and Arkham data.

On Nov. 5, with ether trading near $2,400, Wang sold 6,200 wrapped staked ETH (wstETH) for $18.5 million in USDC. Wrapped staked ETH is a version of Lido's staked ether (stETH) that can be used in DeFi protocols.

This week, ether traded about 14% lower than on the day of the sale, and Wang started buying it back.

He withdrew 5,246 ETH from Binance hot wallets in two transfers of 3,996 ETH and 1,250 ETH. The purchase cost him $10.78 million, so about $7.7 million from the Nov. 5 sale is still left in USDC, according to Lookonchain.

Wang then sent 3,996 ETH ($8.23 million) to Lido. Lido is the largest liquid staking protocol on Ethereum. He deposited the wstETH he received into Spark, a lending protocol in the Sky ecosystem, which was previously known as MakerDAO. In Spark, wstETH can be used as collateral, while it continues to earn staking rewards.

He started out arbitraging Bitcoin between overseas exchanges and China's Taobao and later co-founded F2Pool. He launched the pool in 2013 together with Mao Shixing. At that time, F2Pool was one of the first Bitcoin mining pools in China.

In 2015, he bought a condo in Pattaya for 2,900 BTC. He later sold it for 7 BTC, as the price of Bitcoin had risen many times over.

In 2025, Wang also funded and commanded Fram2, the first crewed spaceflight over Earth's polar regions, on a SpaceX Crew Dragon.

F2Pool also mined ether until Ethereum moved to proof-of-stake in September 2022. Before that, Wang launched the non-custodial staking service stakefish. According to Wang, he decided to do it after a conversation with Vitalik Buterin in 2017. Stakefish was also one of the first node operators of Lido.

Ether is down more than 15% since the start of the month amid outflows from spot ETFs, according to SoSoValue. At press time, ETH is trading at $2,020.

#5204. NEAR Intents fuels $2.7M in Q4 buybacks as revenue surges

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

NEAR Protocol [NEAR] is in the news for all the good reasons. According to reports, the decentralized finance (DeFi) ecosystem of NEAR is seeing increased activity through its cross-chain swap product, NEAR Intents, which has generated enough revenue to fund nearly $2.7 million worth of NEAR token buybacks in the Q4 of 2026.

Interestingly, this figure is already close to the $3.07 million spent on buybacks in Q3 2026, although half of Q4 is still left. So for those unaware, the buyback program began in February 2026. Since then, the protocol has purchased approximately $8 million worth of NEAR tokens.

In fact, so far now, NEAR Intents has collected approximately $8.68 million in fees since the 1st of October. Of this amount, around $2.82 million has been retained by the protocol, while the remaining $5.86 million has gone to solvers and partner applications.

According to Token Terminal data, revenue started around $315K in December 2025, climbed to a first peak of roughly $740K in February 2026, then fell sharply to around $200-240K between April and July, before rebuilding through the autumn to $650K in September. Finally, it crossed the $1 million mark in October.

This means that NEAR Intents is the main revenue driver, contributing roughly 74% of October’s total and the bulk of revenue in nearly every month, while the base protocol’s contribution stays relatively small and steady at around $30-260K.

That said, the platform recorded $3.76 billion in swap volume over the past 30 days, taking its cumulative volume since launch to approximately $37.3 billion. However, sustained buyback activity does not necessarily guarantee price appreciation.

The recent price action of NEAR trading at ​​$3.51 after a drop of 2.6% in the past 24 hours reflected the same. At the same time, the NEAR ETFs also saw outflows worth $5.74 million in the month of November so far.

Yet despite these weak market dynamics, the total value locked (TVL) in NEAR Intents’ confidential pools stood at $140.79 million at press time, up 402.7% over the last 90 days.

That said, the TVL started around $27 million in mid-August and grew steadily through early September, then surged sharply in mid-to-late September, peaking at roughly $185 million around 25-26 September.

After that peak, it pulled back and settled into a fairly stable range of about 135 million to 165 million through October and early November, with a minor dip near the end of October. In fact, according to a recent report by AMBCrypto, NEAR Intents recorded $10.41 billion in Q3 trading volume, up 66% quarter-on-quarter and 891% year-on-year.

This, however, coincided with North Korean hackers linked to the $6.3 billion Binance exploit using six memecoins created on NEAR to launder stolen cryptocurrency, moving approximately 117,000 NEAR worth $430,000.

#5203. Finland Turns Bitcoin Miners Into Discounted Home-Heating

Home » Bitcoin (BTC) » Finland Turns Bitcoin Miners Into Discounted Home-Heating

In the town of Nokia, Finland, a 180-square-meter house now relies entirely on Bitcoin mining rigs for warmth.

During the frigid months of winter in Northern Europe, heating efficiency is the main topic of discussion even in the richest neighborhoods. The frequently hostile surroundings have urged heating specialists to come up with new, creative ways to replace traditional oil heating with cutting-edge technology.

That’s where Bitcoin (BTC) comes into play: Finnish homes have already adapted liquid-cooled mining machine heaters. They utilize the gains equipped from Bitcoin (BTC) mining venues to offset the costs of heating. On the other hand, that kind of solution is highly-dependent on the hash-rate difficulty across the network, among factors like general market conditions.

The next BTC block-reward halving comes in 2028, which could arguably cap the profits of this kind of business if Bitcoin (BTC) crosses $135,000. With installation costs exceeding $20,000, SunBit’s Bitcoin Boiler is aimed at relieving high-heating costs for the residents of Finland. The machine is supposed to work in-profit as long as Bitcoin’s price stays above $70,000.

Some people use Bitcoin mining to make money.In Finland, people are using it to heat their homes.A Finnish startup called SunBit has installed Bitcoin mining machines that double as home heating systems.The concept is pretty wild.Instead of letting the heat generated by… pic.twitter.com/9S4hqTYSl6

For instance, in a historical house in Tampere, Finland’s third-largest city, mining rigs replaced wood burning, maintaining a 1,500-liter water tank between 50°C and 60°C. Sunbit’s liquid-cooled Bitcoin miner utilizes the Antminer S21 XP Hydro, employing a hash rate of up to 495T. The device consumes 5.3 kilowatts of power & draws about 46,400 kWh annually.

The Bitcoin Boiler device stores this heat in water, reaching temperatures up to 60 degrees Celsius, which is then circulated throughout the house via radiators and underfloor heating pipes. It costs roughly $5,200 a year to run. SunBit founder Elmeri Suomi stated: “The heating function itself is unaffected by market conditions; only the payback period changes.”

🇫🇮 🔥 Underground Bitcoin mining in Finland is used to heat its towns, instead of being wasted. pic.twitter.com/EHOfxcWq6M

Bitcoin-based heating systems have first been tested in the Netherlands, but Finland’s adoption mark has grown way further. The Bitcoin Boiler service fee is optional and includes 24/7 remote monitoring with automatic heating strategy optimization aligned with Finland’s floating electricity prices. This runs in a companion app with grid peak-shaving options for advanced enthusiasts.

#5202. Solana Company Reports $3.4M Quarterly Revenue as Staking Business Expands

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Solana Company reported $3.4 million in third-quarter revenue, mostly from staking. Its results show how validator operations can add an operating business to a corporate SOL treasury.

Solana Company is beginning to report more of the economics behind its infrastructure strategy. In its November 11 earnings release, the Nasdaq-listed company said revenue reached $3.4 million in the quarter ended September 30, compared with $697,000 a year earlier.

The question for shareholders is how much of that business can grow beyond the size of the company's own token holdings. A balance sheet gives exposure to SOL; an operating validator business adds a different set of customers, costs and performance measures.

The company reported approximately 2.4 million SOL across its holdings categories at quarter-end. It earned 30,800 SOL in quarterly staking rewards and restaked them. Its Pacific Backbone infrastructure had about 1.3 million SOL delegated, including roughly one million from third parties.

Those figures describe different relationships with tokens. Treasury assets belong to the company, while third-party delegations represent outside stake using its infrastructure. Combining the two into a single ownership figure would overstate the company's treasury.

NewsBTC's coverage of DeFi Development Corp's SOL accumulation provides useful context on the distinction between growing a token balance and creating value for each share.

The company's February infrastructure roadmap set out plans to connect Seoul, Tokyo, Singapore and Hong Kong through a low-latency validator network. Its intended customers included trading firms, exchanges and institutional partners.

The stated business logic was to bring more infrastructure work in-house while developing services that outside customers could use. That provides a useful baseline for judging the latest update: the original plan involved building an activity that could generate fees as well as supporting treasury staking.

The programme also envisaged additional liquidity and execution services. Those earlier ambitions should be assessed separately from what subsequent financial reports demonstrate. A roadmap is useful for tracking delivery, but it cannot substitute for revenue, customers and operating margins.

Solana's official staking documentation explains that token holders delegate stake to validators that process transactions and participate in running the network. Validators may retain a commission from rewards to cover their work and costs.

This structure creates two routes to an operating benefit. A company can earn rewards on its own delegated tokens, and a validator operator can earn commissions for serving other holders. The economics depend on performance, expenses and the amount of stake retained.

The documentation also makes a useful accounting distinction: staking increases are measured in SOL units. Their value in dollars moves with SOL's market price. More tokens therefore do not automatically mean a higher fiat value at the end of a reporting period.

Our assessment is that third-party delegation makes the business more interesting to analyse, because it introduces demand from customers outside the treasury. It also makes the reporting more demanding. Investors need to distinguish rewards earned on company assets from fees earned on client stake and then compare both with operating costs.

Capital structure remains important. Issuing shares can expand a treasury while changing the amount of token exposure attributable to each shareholder. Cash expenses can also require financing even when reported token holdings rise.

Custody and access are additional parts of the picture, as NewsBTC examined in Solmate's disclosure of its SOL custody arrangements. Subsequent reports showing delegation retention, fee income and cash expenditure will help establish whether Solana Company's infrastructure adds durable earnings alongside its asset exposure.

written by the News Desk and edited by Samuel Rae.

#5201. Binance Wallet to End Support for Ethereum Layer 2 Blast

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

In a recent announcement, Binance Wallet stated it will discontinue support for the Blast chain effective November 30, 2026, at 10:00 (UTC). After this date, the crypto exchange said that users will no longer be able to view, send, or receive Blast assets within the Binance Wallet.

Ethereum layer-2 network Blast announced in October 2026 that it was shutting down, a little over two years after its launch, as fading activity left it unable to cover operating costs.

Binance noted that this change only affects the display and management of Blast assets within Binance Wallet, as users can still claim their assets on the official Blast site. In light of this, users are strongly advised to manage or transfer their Blast assets before November 30, 2026, at 10:00 (UTC).

This update only affects the Blast network and does not impact other blockchains supported by Binance Wallet.

Binance has resumed its cryptocurrency listing activity after a brief pause, introducing new perpetual contracts for Hyperliquid (HYPE) and TRON (TRX).

The late-October hack had interrupted the exchange's listings; its last addition was on October 29, when it announced it was listing the Predict token.

In the last 24 hours, Binance has made two listing announcements: one for HYPE and TRX perpetual contracts, and one for TradFi perpetual contracts on Nu Holdings Ltd. Class A Ordinary Shares and Freedom Holding Corp. Common Stock.

Binance Futures will launch HYPEUSDC and TRXUSDC Perpetual Contracts with up to 75x leverage on November 16, 2026, at 08:30 (UTC) and 08:35 (UTC), respectively. The move is intended to expand the list of trading choices offered on Binance Futures and enhance users’ trading experience.

To expand the list of trading choices offered on Binance Futures and enhance users’ trading experience, the NUUSDT USDT-Priced Perpetual Contract and the FRHCUSDT USDT-Priced Perpetual Contract have been admitted to trading on Binance RIE and to clearing and settlement by Binance RCH today, November 12, 2026.

#5200. TSMC confirms data breach as hacker demands Bitcoin worth $215K — Details

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

2026 hasn’t been a great year as far as security is concerned. Whether it is Bitget or Binance, every single episode this year has simply highlighted how important it is.

It hasn’t been a great time for chipmakers around the world either. The latest news associated with Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company or TSMC is a case in point.

TSMC is in the news today after hackers reportedly offered up private information linked to one of the company’s employees on an underground forum. According to the hacker, they have access to over 46,000 records right now. These records will be released publicly if the hackers aren’t paid 3 BTC or $214.7K within one week.

Here, it’s worth pointing out that the hacker in question isn’t exactly holding the information to ransom. At the time of writing, there hadn’t been any demands or efforts to blackmail. Instead, the hacker is looking for a buyer, any buyer, for the information in question.

For its part, TSMC acknowledged that “data of some current and former employees was disclosed without authorization.” However, the company was also quick to stress that there is no real evidence that any of its security systems were compromised. Its preliminary investigative report suggested that,

… this data matches the data in the system service of a certain cloud service provider.

TSMC also clarified that the data in question wasn’t associated or linked with any clients or production.

Right now, the popular chipmaker is working with cloud service providers and cybersecurity agencies to better understand what happened. It is also taking measures to make sure this doesn’t happen again.

Here, it is worth clarifying that the present case isn’t exactly a traditional ransomware attack. Instead, it’s a simple case of data breach since no malware was used and TSMC hasn’t been locked out of its system.

Still, this is an industry that isn’t exactly new to ransomware episodes. Back in 2023, for example, TSMC was targeted by the LockBit group who demanded $70M.

According to Black Kite’s ransomware report, for instance, there has been a 24% YoY increase in ransomware disclosures compared to the previous disclosure period. It also found,

The threat actor ecosystem grew right alongside the victim count, reaching 127 active groups by the close of the period and 146 by June 2026.

Finally, the report in question also revealed that the manufacturing sector accounted for around 22% of all ransomware attacks. Professional, scientific, and medical services accounted for another 18% of the total share. The ransomware breach that targeted DaVita Inc. back in 2025 and affected over 2.7M patients is perhaps the most famous example of the same.

The timing is interesting. Especially since news of this incident coincided with Nvidia hogging the headlines after CEO Jensen Huang resigned following an extramarital scandal. In fact, one can argue that the news has taken the spotlight off TSMC for now.

As expected, this has had a corresponding effect on the sector, related industries, and their performances. For instance, the likes of IREN, Core Scientific, and TeraWulf all fell on the charts in the hours following the aforementioned resignation.
