As for volume, it is currently on a downward trend, which leaves the door open for a potential recovery in the medium term.

The RSI is also in a weak position and may soon reach oversold territory. This could leave room for a recovery sooner than expected, though a test of the aforementioned 200 EMA remains the main scenario. An oversold reading alone wouldn't guarantee that.

Unfortunately, the market is brutal right now and clearly shows a serious level of pressure. A bounce from the longer-term average would be a good sign, but the confirmation of a market recovery would only come if we witness higher highs and sustained buying.

Meanwhile, the price has begun to follow a similar trend and has even fallen below the range of the previous zone of active buying, which was observed on August 18, 2026. It is now at 0.14, which coincides with the previous accumulation point as far back as May 2026.

The RSI trend is also bearish, and we may not see a recovery until a catalyst emerges in the broader market. Despite the RSI being in the oversold zone, there is no strong catalyst to help it rebound quickly, and the moving averages are also beginning to diverge.

This is an indication that the price will most likely continue to fall. Conversely, when the moving averages converge, we should expect a rise in volatility, which would be a more positive development for this asset than any other scenario.

Meanwhile, Chainlink's struggles in the market continue. The price has already broken below the 200-period moving average, and it's now in something of a free fall. LINK is trading at around $9.89, with the asset losing the $10 level as the decline continues.

The next support area in this scenario is around $8.20; below this, another potential support sits near $7. There are earlier trading ranges between the current price and those levels, so the path down isn't necessarily clear. However, a drop towards the lower level would essentially invalidate all of the gains LINK has seen since June.

Overall, the situation isn't much different from that of other tokens. If we overlay the charts for Stellar, Chainlink, Ethereum and others, we'll get roughly the same picture: recovery attempts followed by renewed pressure.

The difference is that with LINK, we might see an additional spike in volatility that quickly weakens the possibility of a rapid rebound, as the moving averages are only just converging. Such a move would be especially damaging if sellers remain in control. When a downtrend is accelerating, the price drop can become critical.

Overall, positive factors for LINK are quite hard to find right now. Despite the fact that the RSI is already approaching the oversold zone, LINK could still find a way to drop even lower.

A recovery is unlikely until a catalyst emerges in the broader market, with higher highs and sustained buying needed to show that the asset is finally moving towards a proper recovery.

Although Cardano usually follows the rest of the cryptocurrency market, here we see a situation similar to the three other tokens we have already discussed. The asset has already broken a fairly strong support that formed around $0.20, and the next potential buying area is now under substantial pressure, being tested around $0.17.

Apart from that, Cardano has already dropped below the halfway point of the recovery from June to October. Despite the RSI already showing oversold conditions, we aren't seeing much room for a bounce here.

On the positive side, volume is clearly moving down and isn't adding strength to the downtrend, which is a substantial advantage. We should also keep in mind that without a bullish catalyst, we most likely won't see a quick recovery here.

There is even a slight increase in volume compared with the beginning of November, hinting at the possibility that bears still have enough strength to pull the price down. The moving averages could come together closer to the next support, which might become the basis for a faster price surge. However, this is not guaranteed at this point.

Overall, Cardano has been in a downtrend since 2025, so the local spikes we could see from July to October shouldn't be taken as a guaranteed sign that the asset is recovering. Even though the price previously broke above the 200-day moving average, that wasn't enough to keep the recovery going.

Cardano might eventually move above it again and go further, but that is not guaranteed either.

#5169. Avalanche Won Over the NYSE. Now It Has to ‘Capture Value’ for AVAX

Avalanche Won Over the NYSE. Now It Has to ‘Capture Value’ for AVAX

The New York Stock Exchange this week selected Avalanche to provide infrastructure for a tokenized securities platform it's prepping. Experts say that it's a big deal for Avalanche's reputation, but warn that it may not translate into an uptick in value for the network's AVAX token.

On Monday, Ava Labs announced Avalanche would serve as the "core ledger technology" for a tokenized securities platform developed by NYSE parent company Intercontinental Exchange. The product will be designed to allow 24/7 trading, clearing, and settlement of tokenized securities, subject to regulatory approval.

Carlos Guzman, research analyst at market maker GSR, told Decrypt it’s “definitely a big win for the project.”

“Having NYSE choose its tech for settlement is a big institutional credibility boost, and it validates the benefits blockchain tech can provide to traditional financial plumbing with instant settlement and programmability,” he said.

Luther Maday, head of North America at crypto payments firm Mercuryo, told Decrypt that the decision came after a year of testing on Avalanche, which signals the network passed checks on "performance, reliability, and control."

"NYSE has chosen the technology for the clearing and settlement layer, which is a much deeper use of blockchain than simply issuing a tokenized asset on a network," Maday added.

Avalanche's AVAX token has risen 3.6% in the past day to $8.64, according to Decrypt data. But experts warn that providing infrastructure doesn't necessarily mean a long-term increase in token value.

"NYSE will be using Avalanche’s infrastructure for clearing and settlement, so the actual trading will still happen off-chain," Guzman explained.

This means NYSE activity will run on dedicated Avalanche infrastructure rather than the public C-Chain, where most of the network's activity happens.

"I would be careful about translating the news directly into expectations for activity on the public C-Chain," Maday said.

That said, Maday said to keep an eye on the details of how the AVAX token is used within the dedicated infrastructure. He also suggested that stablecoin liquidity, validator activity, and network fees could see a boost.

The NYSE has also teamed up with Blockchain.com to sell tokenized stocks and ETFs to crypto investors, while its parent company ICE is working with OKX on a 24/7 tokenized stock venue under an SEC exemption. Morgan Stanley, meanwhile, has started tokenizing securities of its own.

Not every company wants in, though. All seven of the so-called "Magnificent Seven" stocks—Apple, Meta, and the like—opted out of having their shares tokenized on the OKX-ICE venue, along with dozens of other companies.

Even if tokenization does take off, that doesn't guarantee profits for AVAX investors.

"The main question for Avalanche is whether it can do what other blockchains have struggled to do: capture value," Misha Putiatin, founder of collateral markets platform Symbiotic, told Decrypt. "Avalanche has always had an unusually outcome-focused team for this industry, so I suspect they have plans for that, but it remains to be seen."

Ava Labs did not immediately respond to Decrypt's request for comment.

#5168. Google’s new AI reveals hidden genetic clues in new immune-cell test

AlphaGenome 2 tailors predictions to cell types and states, with DeepMind reporting benchmark gains and reproduction of established disease biology.

Google DeepMind announced AlphaGenome 2 on Nov. 11, 2026, a DNA prediction model designed to show how genetic changes act in specific cell types and states. The approach aims to expose disease-linked effects that broad tissue measurements can obscure, giving scientists a more focused starting point for experiments.

The original AlphaGenome produced fixed parallel outputs across tissues and experiments from a million-letter DNA sequence. AlphaGenome 2 instead takes a target condition, such as a tissue, cell type or cell state, alongside the sequence and uses that condition throughout its architecture, according to DeepMind.

Its announcement pairs a reported improvement on an immune-cell benchmark with a computational reproduction of an established bowel-disease mechanism. The examples show how the model could focus laboratory work on particular variants and cells.

That allows predictions to ask how the same stretch of DNA behaves in different cellular settings. DeepMind says this can help identify effects confined to particular cells that broader tissue models miss. A tissue-level result can provide useful context while still leaving the cell responsible for a disease-linked effect unresolved.

AlphaGenome 2 combines DNA with a target cell type or state to predict effects in that setting.

Cell-specific predictions can focus experiments on effects broad tissue averages may miss.

Company-reported research predictions. The ETS2 mechanism predates this model. AlphaGenome 2 has not been validated or approved for clinical or diagnostic use.

Qualitative input-to-prediction schematic using the reported model design, plus DeepMind's retrospective ETS2 example. Shapes, distances and cell symbols encode relationships, not measured signal magnitudes, cell counts or effect sizes.

Company-reported research predictions. The ETS2 mechanism predates this model. AlphaGenome 2 has not been validated or approved for clinical or diagnostic use.

The company reports training the model on bulk and single-cell data from human and mouse samples, including more than 107 million individual cell profiles. The profiles supply training material for learning how DNA activity varies across biological settings.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

DeepMind also says researchers can supply single-cell gene-activity data to condition predictions on newly observed states without retraining the model. The system can then predict properties that were not measured, helping scientists decide which experiments to prioritize.

On TenK10K, a dataset covering 28 primary immune-cell types, DeepMind reports that AlphaGenome 2 identified 30% of causal variants in the evaluation, compared with 16% for the original AlphaGenome fine-tuned on single-cell data.

These developer-reported benchmark results measure variant identification, not diagnostic accuracy or a person’s disease risk.

For researchers, the practical attraction is a larger set of candidate variants to investigate in the cells where they may act. Connecting a disease-associated genetic signal to a particular variant, target gene and cell type can make the next experiment more focused.

The bowel-disease example makes the distinction concrete. In its Nov. 11 announcement, DeepMind says AlphaGenome 2 computationally reproduced a finding involving a regulatory variant that increases activity of the ETS2 gene in gut macrophages, a type of immune cell.

The model singled out the variant and predicted its selective effect on ETS2 in those macrophages, with other cell types and genes largely unaffected. The useful clue is the combination of variant, gene and cell type: it points to a specific cellular setting for investigating the effect.

The underlying biology predates this model. In a June 7, 2024 report, Imperial College London described research conducted with the Francis Crick Institute and UCL that linked a macrophage-active regulatory region to increased ETS2 activity and inflammatory functions. The biological mechanism was therefore already established; AlphaGenome 2's contribution in this example is reproducing it computationally.

As of the Nov. 11 announcement, DeepMind offered AlphaGenome 2 through its API for non-commercial research, with commercial research in private preview. The company states that both AlphaGenome models are intended for research and have not been validated or approved for clinical or diagnostic purposes.

The immediate opportunity is to choose better-targeted experiments: investigate a genetic change in the cell type or state where its effect is predicted to appear.

#5166. Nvidia Sheds $430B in Two Days as Jensen Huang Faces Calls to Resign

Nvidia Sheds $430B in Two Days as Jensen Huang Faces Calls to Resign

Nvidia has lost about $430 billion in market value in two days, as pressure grows in Washington over CEO Jensen Huang's ties to a businesswoman U.S. intelligence reportedly concluded was a Chinese spy.

Nvidia shares closed down 5.5% at $168.89 on Tuesday, following a 4.3% drop on Monday, according to Yahoo! Finance. That leaves the stock down about 9.5% since Friday's close, wiping out roughly $430 billion in market value, according to Yahoo! Finance data. The sell-off, which saw shares slide as much as 8% on Monday, has wiped out all of Nvidia's gains for 2026. The shares were up 3.8% at $175.23 in premarket trading early on Wednesday.

The independent members of Nvidia's board have retained Joon Kim, a former acting U.S. Attorney for the Southern District of New York, and his firm Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen & Katz to carry out the investigation it announced on Sunday, the company said on Tuesday.

A new statement from the independent members of the NVIDIA Board: https://t.co/uSFDhiohG8

— NVIDIA Newsroom (@nvidianewsroom) November 10, 2026

Sen. Kevin Cramer, a North Dakota Republican on the Senate Banking Committee, called on Huang to step down, telling Politico late Tuesday that the risk of extortion was too great. "The potential for extortion is so tremendous that something as sensitive as Nvidia chips, I just think he's got to go," Cramer said, adding that the board's internal investigation was "fine, but not nearly enough."

Hours earlier, former National Economic Council director Larry Kudlow had urged Huang to resign on his Fox Business show. "Step down, Mr. Huang, right now," Kudlow said, adding that he was "not here to indict" Huang but that he should step aside while the investigations take place.

Cramer's committee has jurisdiction over bills that would curb Nvidia chip sales to China, including the AI Overwatch Act and the Chip Security Act, which are being considered for inclusion in the annual defense spending bill, Politico reported.

Senate Democratic leader Chuck Schumer, meanwhile, accused President Donald Trump of failing to act after being briefed on the relationship. "What a dereliction of duty," Schumer said on the Senate floor on Tuesday, according to CNBC. "He's supposed to protect America, not China, not his friends."

Rep. John Moolenaar (R-MI), who chairs the House Select Committee on China, said he wanted to know whether Huang shared privileged information with Alice Huang and whether he was influenced to lobby against chip export restrictions. "If it becomes less of an investigation, more of damage control, that's not going to work," Moolenaar told CNBC. House Foreign Affairs Committee Chairman Brian Mast told ABC News he was prepared to subpoena Huang unless he responded by Wednesday to a request for a closed-door briefing on November 18, the day after Nvidia reports quarterly earnings.

Not everyone in Washington wants the scandal to dominate. Rep. Jay Obernolte (R-CA), co-chair of the House AI task force, told CNBC that a personal scandal should not "interfere" with the policy debate over chip exports to China.

Trump has said the FBI is looking into the matter, and that Huang told him he had no idea the woman was a spy.

Alice Huang, who also goes by Huang Le and is not related to Jensen Huang, worked in banking and investment management in China before becoming the controlling shareholder of Singapore-based Megaspeed in March 2023, according to a Reuters review of corporate records and filings.

Megaspeed, which Nvidia lists as a cloud partner, has drawn U.S. scrutiny over whether it helped Chinese firms obtain export-controlled Nvidia chips, but there has been no public finding that it broke the rules, Reuters reported.

China has denied the intelligence allegations, and Reuters said it could not independently verify them. George Chen, a partner at consultancy The Asia Group, told the news agency that Jensen Huang "may also be viewed as a potential victim" if he was targeted without knowing who she was.

#5165. Zcash loses $1,000 as dormant wallets send ZEC coins to exchanges

Kairos Research found that traders exiting bullish positions drove the initial selloff, with fresh token deposits raising the risk of further losses.

Zcash fell below $1,000 as leveraged traders abandoned bullish positions and selling pressure intensified.

Data from CoinMarketCap showed ZEC at approximately $965 on Nov. 12, after a 24-hour low near $953.

The decline has exposed vulnerabilities in ZEC's market structure, with research showing heavy selling across derivatives exchanges and fresh deposits from previously inactive wallets.

A market investigation by Kairos Research identified the rapid closure of a 12,500 ZEC long position on Hyperliquid as a pivotal moment in the selloff.

The position, accumulated in the hour following the release of US inflation data, was closed through market orders within two seconds at 15:29:58 UTC on Nov. 10, just as Bitcoin began retreating.

ZEC subsequently fell from $1,193 to $1,001 as traders sold a net 160,000 ZEC across four perpetual futures exchanges over the following 90 minutes.

Binance spot hourly-closing price represented in the Kairos Research graphic. All readings are approximate, digitized from the line and rounded to the nearest $10; the underlying price observations were not available. The sharp decline occurred Nov. 10, 15:00-17:00 UTC. Price is separate because the import format has no dual axis.

Source: Kairos Research, ZEC market structure and exchange deposits (Nov. 11, 2026) · Kairos Research post, accessible mirror

Binance accounted for 74,000 ZEC, followed by OKX with 42,000, Hyperliquid with 31,000, and Bybit with 13,000. Spot markets recorded approximately 27,000 ZEC in net taker selling.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Kairos found that traders primarily drove the selling by unwinding existing bullish positions rather than forced liquidations.

On Hyperliquid, taker sales associated with closing longs reached 109,000 ZEC between 14:30 and 17:00 UTC, compared with 50,000 ZEC from traders opening shorts. Forced liquidations accounted for only 6,900 ZEC during that period.

Coinbase had already recorded approximately 3,000 ZEC in net spot selling before the breakdown, while derivatives traders on Binance, OKX and Hyperliquid remained net buyers.

The researchers also traced Coinbase deposits and found that only about 12,000 ZEC had arrived across Nov. 9 and 10, suggesting most of the initial spot selling came from inventory already held on the exchange.

However, fresh deposits accelerated after the decline.

By 18:00 UTC on Nov. 11, Coinbase had received another 16,300 ZEC, including 9,800 ZEC from three wallets that had been inactive since withdrawing funds from Coinbase Prime in November and December 2025.

Those wallets still held approximately 19,000 ZEC, while Binance received another 8,600 ZEC in large deposits over the same period.

The combined 24,900 ZEC in new exchange deposits raises the prospect of additional spot selling, although transfers alone do not establish an intention to liquidate.

Kairos Research image labels show approximate combined daily deposits to Coinbase, Coinbase Prime and Binance, including the separately marked Grayscale settlement. Amounts are rounded to the nearest 1,000 ZEC, not exact ledger totals. Nov. 11 is partial and covers deposits before 18:00 UTC. Exchange deposits do not establish sales.

Source: Kairos Research, ZEC market structure and exchange deposits (Nov. 11, 2026) · Kairos Research post, accessible mirror

The selling pressure has also extended to Grayscale's Zcash ETF, ZCSH, which is heading toward its second consecutive week of net withdrawals.

Data from SoSoValue showed that the fund recorded approximately $13 million in outflows during the week ending Nov. 6, followed by another $17 million this week.

The combined withdrawals are approaching $30 million.

The fund's cumulative net inflows have fallen to $192 million, while total net assets have also declined to $562 million from about $772 million at the end of the previous week.

However, Kairos found no evidence that fresh ETF redemptions caused the initial Nov. 10 selloff.

ZCSH traded 2.46 million shares that day without recording new creations or redemptions, while 1,861 ZEC transferred into Coinbase Prime represented settlement of an earlier withdrawal.

In a research report, the asset manager said Zcash's monthly active developer population had roughly doubled over two years, while developer participation in the broader cryptocurrency industry has retreated from its previous peak.

The firm also found that approximately 70% of Zcash's active developers had more than one year of cryptocurrency development experience, compared with 58% across the industry.

The bullish outlook also reflects progress in the network's development.

Zcash activated its NU7 upgrade on Nov. 6, reducing its target block interval from 75 seconds to 25 seconds and introducing changes designed to improve private payment processing.

Meanwhile, data from Zcash's shielded supply tracker showed that approximately 5 million ZEC, representing more than 29% of circulating supply, was held in shielded pools as of Nov. 10.

Grayscale has reinforced its bullish thesis with a $1 million commitment to the Zcash ecosystem, including $400,000 already awarded to four development and advocacy organizations.

The firm also launched a nationwide advertising campaign featuring a prominent Wall Street Journal promotion and a dedicated investment education platform.

It intends to reinvest sponsor fees collected from ZCSH into network development, marketing, and education for up to the fund's first year as an exchange-traded product.

Nonetheless, Grayscale's investments and Zcash's growing developer community have yet to reverse the deterioration in investor demand. With the fund approaching $30 million in withdrawals over two weeks and previously inactive wallets still holding another 19,000 ZEC, renewed selling could further test the cryptocurrency's ability to recover above $1,000.

#5161. XRP proposed lending vaults lock deposits and expose holders to risk

Proposed XRPL lending rules would lock XRP vault withdrawals during investment periods, exposing depositors to borrower repayment risk.

Proposed V1.1 rules for the XRP Ledger (XRPL) would require new loan brokers to use closed-ended native-lending vaults, forgoing withdrawals during the investment period and leaving depositors with shares exposed to borrower repayments.

In a Nov. 12 snapshot, XRPLWin showed LendingProtocol and LendingProtocolV1_1 each at 22 of 35 trusted-validator votes, with SingleAssetVault at 23 of 35. The explorer displayed a 29-vote threshold, and XRPL’s amendment rules require more than 80% support maintained for two weeks.

Under Ripple’s proposed V1.1 transaction rules, newly created loan brokers would have to attach to closed-ended vaults. Brokers created before that amendment activates could continue originating loans against open-ended vaults.

During subscription, depositors can add assets and withdraw, while both actions are blocked and capital can fund loans during investment. Redemption reopens withdrawals, while new deposits and new loans are barred.

The lending mechanism transfers requested principal to the borrower, excluding fees. Once XRP is lent, the depositor holds a claim through vault shares while the borrower controls the disbursed tokens.

A deposit made with already-owned XRP does not establish a market purchase, and an investment-period withdrawal lock also does not permanently immobilize the XRP passed to borrowers.

The amount held idle in vaults, the amount outstanding on loan, and what borrowers do with it would have to be measured separately before drawing conclusions about tradable supply.

XRPL’s design uses fixed-term, uncollateralized loans and off-chain underwriting, so holders take on exposure to borrowers’ ability to repay and the broker’s credit decisions.

In an XRP lending vault, borrower-paid XRP interest could increase the XRP claim represented by those shares, while losses could reduce it. That keeps the investment denominated in XRP without guaranteeing the number of tokens ultimately recovered.

A broker may deposit the same asset in a separate account to buffer defaults, but that protection is optional and configurable. Its presence does not guarantee repayment or eliminate depositor losses.

V1.1 would also put newly created vaults on cash-basis accounting, with interest becoming income when paid. Vaults created under the earlier whole-life model, which books scheduled interest at loan origination, would retain that accounting permanently.

The vault must permit transfers, and a recipient must be eligible to hold both the shares and the underlying asset. Private-vault credentials and applicable asset restrictions can prevent a transfer.

Even where ownership can move, a sale requires a willing buyer and an executable price.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Proposed V1.1 rules for new loan brokers would separate the depositor’s withdrawal rights from control of loan principal.

Deposits and withdrawals are open before the investment period.

Deposits and withdrawals are blocked while capital can fund loans.

Withdrawals reopen; no new deposits or new loans. Recovery depends on the vault’s assets.

An investment-period withdrawal lock does not permanently remove loaned XRP from circulation.

Native lending remains unactivated in the November 12 snapshot. The design shown is for closed-ended XRP vaults and new V1.1 brokers; legacy brokers can retain open-ended vaults. Credit cover is optional. Transferability does not establish a buyer or price, and redemption does not guarantee full recovery.

Source-bound mechanism diagram, without token quantities or a yield forecast. Selectable phases reproduce documented permissions. Connectors indicate claims and token transfers, not proportional balances.

Native lending remains unactivated in the November 12 snapshot. The design shown is for closed-ended XRP vaults and new V1.1 brokers; legacy brokers can retain open-ended vaults. Credit cover is optional. Transferability does not establish a buyer or price, and redemption does not guarantee full recovery.

Arf’s Oct. 22 announcement with Ripple noted that its first phase would provide direct RLUSD working capital on XRPL, with a planned move to native lending infrastructure after amendment activation. Underwriting would remain off-chain, and RLUSD would be the asset disbursed and repaid.

For holders, the next tests are amendment activation, funded XRP vaults, borrower drawdowns, paid interest, defaults and repayments.

Successful share sales would separately show whether an early exit is usable. Until those outcomes are observable, the proposed bargain for a closed-ended XRP vault is a withdrawal commitment in exchange for credit exposure, with borrower performance determining the income.

#5160. Kraken Parent Payward Brings 200 UK and EU Stocks Onchain as xStocks

Kraken Parent Payward Brings 200 UK and EU Stocks Onchain as xStocks

Payward, the parent company of crypto exchange Kraken, has tokenized more than 200 UK and European stocks through its xStocks platform, it announced on Wednesday.

The first batch covers some of the most actively traded companies listed in the UK and the European Union, with more to be added on a rolling basis. Like other xStocks, each token is backed 1:1 by the underlying share, which is bought and held by a regulated custodian for as long as the token trades, according to Payward.

Payward's @xStocksFi is expanding beyond US tokenized equities.

In partnership with @GTNglobal, more than 200 of the UK and EU’s largest equities are now tokenized by xStocks, including HSBC, Shell, LVMH and Airbus.

The first international launch, with more markets to follow.…

The tokens are available to trade on Kraken and through other platforms in the xStocks Alliance, and on blockchains where xStocks already trade, including Solana, Ethereum and TON. They are offered in more than 110 markets, but remain unavailable to investors in the UK.

"A portfolio should not have borders or trading hours," said Mark Greenberg, Payward's chief commercial officer. "Tokenizing every other international market is the next step."

The launch takes xStocks past 1,500 tokenized stocks, ETFs and IPOs. The platform holds more than $1.1 billion in assets, has processed over $45 billion in total transaction volume, and has more than 550,000 holders, Payward said.

The new tokens are the first delivered under Payward's partnership with GTN, a brokerage infrastructure firm that handles execution and custody of the underlying shares. When the two partnered in July, Payward said it planned to start with Hong Kong-listed stocks; hundreds of Hong Kong listings will now follow in the coming weeks, the company said.

The expansion also delivers on part of Payward's partnership with the London Stock Exchange, announced in September, under which Payward agreed to tokenize the 100 largest London-listed companies. Subject to regulatory approval, the exchange intends to list xStocks and begin trading them on LSE 24, its planned near-24-hour trading venue, in 2027.

Payward has been drawing interest from traditional exchanges. Nasdaq agreed to invest $100 million in the company at a $21 billion valuation in September, building on a March partnership to bring tokenized stocks to market in 2027, while Deutsche Börse bought a $200 million stake in April.

The company has also been expanding through acquisitions, agreeing to buy derivatives exchange Bitnomial for $550 million and Lithuania's Saldo Bank as part of a push into the EU.

#5157. Bitcoin and Ethereum sink to 3-month lows, but a bigger crypto selloff may be ahead

A $727 million liquidation wave has exposed the market's fragility, with Bitcoin facing a critical $68,000 support test and deeper historical downside risks.

Bitcoin and Ethereum fell to three-month lows Thursday, deepening a cryptocurrency selloff that has erased $550 billion in market value since October.

Bitcoin slumped to $71,317, its lowest level since August, while Ethereum briefly fell below $2,000 to touch $1,983. The declines have pushed the broader crypto market's valuation from approximately $2.94 trillion in early October to $2.39 trillion.

The downturn has coincided with a sharp deterioration in market sentiment. CryptoQuant's Analyst Consensus Index fell from -1.0 to -1.8 standard deviations over seven days, signaling increasingly bearish expectations as investors grapple with persistent selling pressure and uncertainty over the market's next direction.

The latest price decline triggered widespread losses across cryptocurrency derivatives markets as leveraged traders were caught on the wrong side of the downturn.

According to CoinGlass, 125,731 traders were liquidated over 24 hours, with closed positions totaling about $727 million.

Bitcoin accounted for $293.8 million of those liquidations, with bullish positions representing 91.1% of the total. Ethereum followed with $120.2 million, of which 87.7% came from traders betting on higher prices.

Together, Bitcoin and Ethereum accounted for approximately $414 million, or 57% of the market-wide total.

Bitcoin and Ethereum accounted for about $414 million of the roughly $727 million market total, according to a rolling 24-hour CoinGlass snapshot on Nov. 12.

The selling intensified during a four-hour period when nearly $489 million in leveraged positions were liquidated. Long positions accounted for approximately $446 million of those losses, compared with $44 million in short positions.

The largest individual liquidation was a $31 million Bitcoin position on decentralized derivatives exchange Hyperliquid.

The concentration of losses among bullish traders illustrates how the price decline forced exchanges to close leveraged positions, adding to selling pressure as the market struggled to find support.

The downturn comes during weakening institutional demand and contracting liquidity across the crypto market.

Data from SoSoValue showed that US spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds have recorded approximately $2 billion in net outflows over the past three weeks. Ethereum-focused products are also heading toward a seventh consecutive week of withdrawals, with more than $2.1 billion leaving the funds during that period.

The sustained withdrawals have coincided with a broader contraction in available crypto liquidity.

The supply of major dollar-linked stablecoins contracted by $14.2 billion over 30 days, while Bitcoin's apparent demand declined by 284,000 BTC.

These developments suggest that the latest liquidation wave struck a market already struggling to attract fresh capital, leaving fewer sources of demand to absorb the selling pressure.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Beyond the immediate selling pressure, Bitcoin's deteriorating technical structure and historical valuation indicators point to the market could face further losses before establishing a durable bottom.

The cryptocurrency has fallen below its 200-day moving average, which stood near $73,200, breaking a widely followed trend indicator used to assess the broader direction of prices.

The breakdown has shifted attention toward lower price levels where traders expect buying interest to emerge.

According to CoinGlass, Bitcoin's liquidation heatmap identifies approximately $68,000 as the next potential support region. A decline toward that level would extend the current downturn and test whether buyers can absorb additional selling pressure.

However, historical on-chain valuation data shows that Bitcoin could face a considerably deeper correction if market conditions continue to deteriorate.

Joao Wedson, chief executive of blockchain analytics firm Alphractal, highlighted Bitcoin's Cumulative Value Days Destroyed (CVDD) indicator, which currently places a historical bear-market valuation reference near $52,700.

The metric uses the value and age of Bitcoin moving on-chain to identify price regions associated with previous market bottoms.

At a Bitcoin price of $72,500, the cryptocurrency was trading approximately 37.7% above the CVDD level. A complete retest would imply a further decline of roughly 27% from that reference price.

The $72,500 comparison price puts CoinGlass's estimated $68,000 liquidation heatmap region about 6.2% lower and Joao Wedson's $52,700 CVDD reading about 27.3% lower.

Source: CoinGlass liquidation heatmap · Alphractal Academy (CVDD methodology)

Wedson pointed to previous market cycles in which Bitcoin approached or briefly traded below the indicator.

In December 2018, Bitcoin bottomed near $3,122 against a CVDD reading of $3,060. Similarly, the November 2022 market bottom occurred around $15,479, slightly below the indicator's $15,900 valuation.

Comparable patterns emerged during the January 2015 and March 2020 downturns, when Bitcoin traded below the metric before eventually recovering.

Still, the historical relationship does not establish that Bitcoin must revisit $52,700 during the current downturn.

Unlike a fixed technical support level, CVDD changes with on-chain activity as older Bitcoin moves between wallets. Its valuation threshold can rise over time, potentially narrowing the gap between the indicator and market prices without requiring an equivalent decline in Bitcoin.

That leaves the market facing two distinct price tests: whether buyers can defend the $68,000 region identified by CoinGlass, and whether a sustained recovery can push Bitcoin back above its 200-day moving average.

A failure to stabilize around those levels would strengthen concerns that the latest selloff is developing into a more prolonged correction, bringing the deeper historical valuation levels highlighted by Wedson into focus.

#5156. DOJ’s Operation TorNado Arrests 354 and Seizes $234 Million in Cash and Crypto

The Justice Department and Europol said Thursday that Operation TorNado has produced 354 arrests and the seizure of more than $234 million in cash and virtual currencies, in what the FBI called the largest takedown yet of dark web drug vendors and buyers.

The announcement came from the department’s Joint Criminal Opioid and Darknet Enforcement team, or JCODE, an FBI-led group created in 2018. Officials said Operation TorNado is the sixth, and most extensive, internationally coordinated action since then, and that it builds on last year’s Operation RapTor. The sweep ran from May 2025 to October 2026.

Europol, which coordinated work with agencies in 14 countries, published a parallel statement the same day. The operation targeted vendors and buyers who had been active on five dark web markets already seized by law enforcement: Archetyp, Bohemia, Nemesis, Kingdom and Tor2Door. Officials also identified accounts on DrugHub and on forums, single-vendor shops and encrypted messaging services.

The seizures included about 5.7 metric tons of illicit drugs worldwide, among them fentanyl and other opioids, methamphetamine, heroin, cocaine, ecstasy and MDMA, plus 38 pill presses and 209 firearms. Neither the Justice Department nor Europol split the $234 million between cash and cryptocurrency, and neither named bitcoin or any other coin in the official totals.

Europol put the European share at more than €60 million in cash and cryptocurrencies, alongside 950 kilograms of drugs and 57 firearms, and the U.S. share at more than €150 million, or $166 million, in cash and cryptocurrencies, plus more than 4.8 metric tons of drugs and 152 firearms. Those regional money figures still combine cash and crypto. Europol said its forensic and blockchain analysis teams traced illicit transactions as part of the work.

The closest official window into how the vendors were paid comes from the cases the Justice Department highlighted and from court papers posted with the announcement.

Alexander Green, 32, of Jacksonville, North Carolina, was arrested in June and charged in the Southern District of Ohio with conspiracy to distribute controlled substances, including fentanyl. The department said he allegedly operated a vendor called STARLABS on DrugHub, which it described as a Monero-only dark web market, and that the group has been linked to two overdose deaths. A criminal complaint says undercover purchases from STARLABS were made “in exchange for cryptocurrency.” If convicted, he faces up to 20 years in prison.

In a separate Florida arrest, brothers Stanislav Chernyshov, 30, and Vladislav Chernyshov, 35, are charged in the Eastern District of Virginia with conspiracy to distribute Schedule I synthetic opioids. Prosecutors say they allegedly ran the vendor “BarbaraWhite,” selling counterfeit oxycodone pills laced with nitazenes and shipping more than 1,000 parcels across 49 states. A search-warrant affidavit describes bitcoin transfers tied to that account and the Nemesis market. They each face up to 20 years if convicted.

Operation TorNado led to 132 arrests in the United States, 59 in the United Kingdom, 45 in Hungary and 41 in Germany, along with arrests in France, New Zealand, the Netherlands, Slovenia, Austria, Ireland, Czechia, Australia and Finland. A number of investigations are still ongoing to identify the people behind remaining dark web accounts, the department said.

FBI Director Kash Patel called Operation TorNado “the largest interagency takedown of Dark Web drug vendors and buyers to date.” IRS Criminal Investigation Chief Jarod Koopman, whose agency was part of the JCODE group, put the financial work at the center: “Criminal networks may operate across borders and in the shadows of the Dark Web, but they cannot operate without money.”

Federal prosecutions are underway in more than 20 districts. An indictment or complaint only alleges that crimes were committed, and the defendants are presumed innocent until proven guilty.

What the announcement does not settle is how much of the $234 million was cryptocurrency, and how much of that was bitcoin. Officials have not said. The record on Operation TorNado shows a combined cash-and-crypto seizure, a Monero-only market in at least one featured case, and bitcoin transfers in court papers, without a breakdown of the total.

#5154. Morning Minute: What Anthropic’s $1.4 Trillion IPO Means for Crypto

Morning Minute: What Anthropic’s $1.4 Trillion IPO Means for Crypto

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

💵 What Anthropic’s $1.4 Trillion IPO Means for Crypto

Several key new details for Anthropic’s pending IPO were released on Tuesday. The IPO price range was set at $70 to $78 a share, selling 825 million Class A shares to raise about $64.4 billion (at the top of the range). Pricing is expected November 18, with trading starting November 19 on Nasdaq under ANT. Roughly 20% of the deal is set aside for retail, which is about double what a book this size usually gives individual investors.

Notably on Hyperliquid, ANT is trading at $1.8T with $100M in daily trading volume and $200M in open interest (a big enough market to provide some signal). So perhaps traders are a bit more bullish on ANT price action than Wall Street.

Alongside the IPO sits a $26 billion private placement of non-voting Class N stock, bought at the IPO price by seven of Anthropic’s own compute suppliers. Google and Nvidia take about $10 billion each, AMD $2.5 billion, Broadcom, Micron and Microsoft about $1 billion apiece, TSMC $500 million. Those shares carry no lock-up. Combined with the IPO, Anthropic walks away with roughly $84.6 billion for general corporate purposes, mostly compute.

Regarding financials, Anthropic just reported revenue of $33.2 billion through nine months against $2.5 billion a year earlier and a $1.26 billion operating loss. It reported a $104 billion net loss, almost all of it a non-cash charge on convertible notes. And $900.9 billion in committed spending, with $70.7 billion due inside twelve months.

The liquidity drain thesis has been circulating since April, when CoinDesk laid out the case that mega-IPOs pull speculative money from the same pool that funds crypto. Retail allocations are the mechanism. A dollar earmarked for an IPO ticket is a dollar not in Bitcoin or memecoins.

We already ran this experiment. SpaceX listed June 12 at more than $2 trillion. Bitcoin fell 29% going into it, bottomed at $58,500 on June 30, and then rose 37% off that low. But notably, the Bitcoin bottom was effectively in on June 5 at $59k, a full week ahead of the SPCX IPO.

So the question is not whether an IPO pulls money out. It’s whether that already happened. Bitcoin fell roughly 15% from its early-October high through a $6.3 billion exchange hack, an AI capex selloff and a Democratic sweep.

Two things make this deal smaller than it looks for crypto. The retail slice is roughly $12.9 billion against SpaceX’s $22 billion, about 59% as much. And the $26 billion private placement never touches public market liquidity at all, because it comes from Anthropic’s vendors, who then get paid back in compute contracts.

Thus, if the SpaceX pattern repeats, and with the ~60% factor, we could expect BTC to drop another 5% or so into the Anthropic IPO (notably, that’s right where the 200-week moving average is). And we would expect the effective bottom to be in sometime in the next 1-2 days.

The good news is, the pain is mostly behind us, in that scenario. We will find out soon enough if IPO history repeats itself for crypto…

#5153. Helius encrypts Solana transfers without removing operator trust

Its documented default prover, the service that constructs zero-knowledge proofs, adds a hardware requirement where secret transaction inputs are processed inside an attested AWS Nitro enclave, an isolated computing environment.

Solana can check that a transfer satisfies the protocol’s rules, but protecting the inputs while that proof is made depends on the prover’s isolation, code, and deployment policies.

The practical question for wallet developers integrating the launch is which of those protections their software can verify.

Helius’s wallet integration guide says proof generation needs plaintext inputs covering the complete transaction data and nullifier keys. It identifies an attested AWS Nitro enclave as the SDKs’ default prover.

The protocol specification lists private inputs including the asset, amount, blinding values, and nullifier secret for notes being spent, along with paths proving those notes belong to the state tree. The proof also includes output assets, amounts, and blinding values.

Those inputs become readable to the proving code inside the enclave, and the documented client protects them from the host operator by encrypting requests to a key held by the attested enclave.

The nullifier secret also derives the markers used to prevent repeat spending. In the native wallet integration, the wallet retains the signing key, and the TypeScript SDK leaves signing of the finished Solana transaction with the application’s signer.

Helius documents local decryption, which keeps the viewing key on the device, and delegated decryption, which shares a viewing key with an authorized provider. Delegation gives that provider read access to balances and history, without granting spending authority.

AWS describes Nitro Enclaves as isolated environments whose memory the parent instance’s processes or administrators cannot access. Enclaves have no persistent storage, interactive access, or external networking.

The tagged SDK refuses a remote prover request without one, before making an HTTP call. A wallet can pin a reviewed release policy or use a network policy authenticated by the SDK’s pinned signers. Signed policies refresh during attestation and expire after use.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Helius’s documented default path sends encrypted proof inputs into an AWS Nitro enclave, then returns the proof to the wallet before transaction signing.

The client verifies attestation against an approved policy before encrypting remote proof requests.

Code review, AWS isolation and the operator’s initial KMS policy binding underpin the documented confidentiality design.

Wallets check an approved enclave and key; code review and deployment policy remain separate dependencies.

Documented default configuration only, not independent live attestation or an audit. Other proving or authorization paths can differ. Native wallet signing, remote proving and balance-viewing access have separate roles.

Qualitative data-flow diagram of the documented native-wallet default prover. No numerical scale or calculated values. The selectable panel distinguishes client attestation checks from code, hardware and deployment trust dependencies.

Documented default configuration only, not independent live attestation or an audit. Other proving or authorization paths can differ. Native wallet signing, remote proving and balance-viewing access have separate roles.

A wallet can automatically check that the enclave matches an approved software image and encryption key, yet deciding which image to approve still requires confidence in the code and its reviewers.

The Nitro deployment guide says the measured image includes the prover, startup script, certificate bundle, allowed external hosts, indexer URL, and KMS key identity. Its design uses AWS Key Management Service to release the shared encryption seed only to matching attested enclaves.

The guide says clients cannot read the KMS key policy and rely on the operator’s deployment for that guarantee. It identifies AWS KMS, AWS Support, Nitro hardware, and the operator as trust roots during initial policy binding.

Even correctly encrypted requests leave metadata visible to the parent, such as paths, queries, timing, and sizes. The path reveals the proof’s circuit shape.

The versioned TEE guide records a mainnet build based on v0.6.0-alpha, with measured-build provenance, distinct from the ts-sdk-v0.8.0-alpha documentation tag.

The specification allows client-side proofs, and the repository supplies localnet tooling and downloadable prover binaries.

Helius’s launch announcement says Certora, zkSecurity, and OtterSec audited programs and circuits. Its security page lists Certora in October and zkSecurity in August, while marking OtterSec as awaiting a report.

The launch gives wallet developers a trust decision on which code and deployment policy they approve to handle proof inputs.

#5149. Jane Street Lifts Its Strategy (MSTR) Stake 54% to 4.1 Million Shares

A 13F filed Thursday shows Jane Street held 4,119,097 Strategy shares as of Sept. 30, up 53.8%.

Jane Street Group raised its Strategy (MSTR) stake 53.8% in the third quarter, to 4,119,097 shares, a Form 13F filed Thursday shows, a position dated to Sept. 30 that does not capture any trades the firm may have made since then.

Those caveats matter as much as the increase. Jane Street is a trading firm, and one of the largest market makers in stocks, ETFs, and options, and the same filing lists call options covering 13,573,000 Strategy shares and put options covering 13,991,800 shares, each more than three times the size of the common stake. Some of that stock is likely a hedge for the options and market-making book rather than a straight bet on the company. The 13F does not say how much.

The shares were valued at $630.6 million in the filing, using the Sept. 30 price of $153.09. Strategy has fallen about 25% since then. At Thursday’s close of $114.41, the same 4,119,097 shares were worth about $471 million.

The Class A position is up 1,441,475 shares from the 2,677,622 Jane Street reported at June 30, when the same holding was valued at $232.8 million. The firm filed about four days ahead of the Nov. 16 deadline; the SEC accepted the report at 4:04 p.m. Eastern on Thursday.

The options lines were already large in the second quarter and grew again. Calls covering 13,573,000 shares were valued at $2.08 billion in the new filing, and puts covering 13,991,800 shares, listed in two rows, were valued at $2.14 billion. Those figures use the same Sept. 30 share price as the common-stock line; a 13F reports the underlying shares the options cover, not the cash premium paid for the contracts.

The report is a holdings filing that folds in positions across seven other included managers, among them Jane Street Capital, Jane Street Options and Jane Street Global Trading, so the Strategy lines can include inventory across the group rather than a single proprietary bet. Authorized Signatory Jeremy Kahn signed the report in New York on Nov. 12. The table lists 14,764 entries valued at $1.19 trillion.

Jane Street’s 53.8% increase was the sharpest percentage increase among the large Strategy stakes in Thursday’s early 13F wave. Capital International Investors, a Capital Group unit, raised its stake 4.5% to 36,467,109 shares, worth about $4.17 billion at Thursday’s close. Invesco added 10.9%, to 10,362,698 shares, worth about $1.19 billion. Renaissance Technologies cut its position 41%, to 1,515,560 shares, worth about $173 million, and left the spot bitcoin ETF holdings it had reported at midyear, including BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust.

JPMorgan Chase, in a filing already reported, lifted its Strategy stake 36% to 848,932 shares and held 11.4 million IBIT shares, worth about $460 million at Thursday’s close. Bank of New York Mellon, in another early 13F, raised its stake in Strive (ASST). Those share counts, like Jane Street’s, are as of Sept. 30.

Still to file by the Nov. 16 deadline are the main quarterly 13Fs from Goldman Sachs, Morgan Stanley, Millennium and Mubadala. Institutional managers that exercise investment discretion over $100 million or more in 13F securities have 45 days after quarter-end to disclose those holdings. That window ran to Nov. 14, a Saturday, so the deadline is Monday.

The Sept. 30 snapshot also precedes this week’s decision by MSCI to remove Strategy from its USA index, a step that will force index funds to sell by Nov. 30, and it precedes the stock’s slide since quarter-end. Monday’s remaining filings will show where other large holders stood on the same date, not where they stand now.

#5146. Anthropic’s new AI leads freelance benchmarks, yet fails 58% of the tasks

Anthropic’s Fable 5.2 tops a freelance work benchmark at 41.3%, meeting professional standards on roughly two in five projects.

Anthropic’s Fable 5.2 ranks first on the Remote Labor Index, a test of complete freelance projects, with 41.3% of its deliverables judged to meet a professional reference standard.

Scale’s Nov. 12 leaderboard lists Fable 5.2 at 41.3%, ahead of the next-highest model, Opus 5.5 at 21.25%. Anthropic reported the result in its Nov. 9 release announcement, labeling the RLI score “High effort” and indicating that safeguards were enabled.

The original RLI paper, submitted in October 2025 by researchers from the Center for AI Safety and Scale AI, describes a dataset of 240 projects across 23 freelance work categories. Each includes a brief, the files needed to complete the task, and a professional human-reference deliverable.

The selected projects include documents, audio, video, 3D models, and computer-aided design files. That range makes file production and finished-product quality part of the challenge, alongside understanding what the client requested.

The researchers’ original evaluation method uses trained human reviewers to inspect the AI’s work against the brief and reference deliverable.

Independent evaluations and majority voting determine outcomes, and a project passes when the AI’s deliverable satisfies the brief at least as well as the reference, at a quality a reasonable client would accept.

A useful prototype or a partial result can therefore fall below the pass threshold, even when it demonstrates relevant skills. Passing demands a deliverable that clears the professional reference standard.

The unit is an accepted benchmark project. The score alone does not establish job losses, deployment cost or human-free execution.

Fable 5.2’s score still leaves most evaluated projects below that acceptance threshold.

The original study excludes work that inherently requires client interaction, teamwork, or physical labor, as well as projects whose outcome must be awaited before it can be evaluated. Those exclusions limit how broadly the result can apply to the wider remote-work economy.

Anthropic’s release page specifies High effort, but does not establish the precise RLI execution setup, spending per project, or amount of human support.

RLI provides a demanding definition of success for a business assessing AI’s usefulness: carrying an assignment through to an inspectable product, with professional work setting the acceptance standard.

#5144. Decrypt Becomes First News Publisher to Offer Perps Trading, Powered by Solana's Phoenix

Decrypt Becomes First News Publisher to Offer Perps Trading, Powered by Solana's Phoenix

What happens when the place you read the news becomes the place you act on it?

The news that moves markets and the tools to trade them have long lived in separate places. Decrypt is bringing them together.

Decrypt is integrating perpetual futures trading, powered by Phoenix, a fully onchain perpetuals exchange on Solana, making it the first media platform to offer perps trading directly alongside its journalism.

Readers will be able to take long or short positions on crypto, stocks and commodities from the same place they follow the news that moves them, without switching apps, connecting external wallets or moving funds between platforms.

"Information influences decisions, shapes opinions and moves markets," said Ilan Hazan, co-founder and COO of Decrypt. "A reader might discover an opportunity through a publication, develop a view and then leave to act on it somewhere else. The publisher monetizes the impression, while someone else monetizes the transaction. It means the publisher captures only a small part of the economic activity that its information and distribution generates."

Rather than treating media and financial products as separate businesses, he said, Decrypt is bringing them together. "We're allowing readers to go from understanding what's happening in the markets to taking a position, without leaving Decrypt.”

"Partnering with Decrypt closes the gap between reading the news and acting on it," said Kabir Sethi, Head of Business Development at Phoenix. "With over 100 Phoenix perpetuals markets built directly into Decrypt, readers can take in a story, form a view, and trade it in the same place. We are excited to help play a part in transforming Decrypt into a true information exchange."

Perpetual futures, or perps, are contracts that let traders bet on an asset's price rising or falling without an expiry date, typically using leverage. Leverage magnifies both gains and losses, and positions can be liquidated quickly when markets move against them.

Phoenix offers markets in crypto, U.S. stocks, gold and pre-IPO companies around the clock, with up to 40x leverage and low fees, according to its website. It is developed by Ellipsis Labs, a team that has built exclusively on Solana and whose CEO, Eugene Chen, has described perps as having "crazy product market fit."

"These are very, very strong, high revenue businesses, probably some of the best in the industry," Chen said of perps exchanges in a podcast interview with Blockworks' Lightspeed earlier this year. Phoenix routes each trade at the moment it lands onchain, splitting it between its order book and market makers "based on what's going to give you the best price," he explained.

Perps trading runs through the Decrypt Money Account, a single self-custodial account on Solana designed to work without seed phrases, gas fees or bridging.

One balance now funds it all: prediction markets on Decrypt Predict, Jupiter-powered token swaps, Earn and, now, perpetual futures. Users can also send funds privately, after Decrypt added confidential transfers through Solana infrastructure firm Helius.

Imagine reading about a public company's earnings and taking a position on its stock, at any hour. Or following a breaking story about Bitcoin and going long or short in the same moment.

From information to execution, in one place.

Announced in September, The Information Exchange is Decrypt's vision for the next generation of financial media: a single destination bringing together news, intelligence, market data, probabilities, direct financial participation—with perps forming the latest building block in that infrastructure.

News moves markets. Now readers can move with it.

Perpetual futures are leveraged products and carry a high risk of loss. Availability may vary by region.

#5142. Solana treasury Forward Industries flashes a 30% SOL discount, with a catch

Forward Industries’ Solana treasury trades at a 29.7% NAV discount that narrows to 2.4% when potential share dilution is counted.

Forward Industries' Nov. 10 update puts the Solana treasury company's shares at a 29.7% discount to net assets using common shares alone.

Counting all the potential shares in the same disclosure, that discount shrinks to 2.4%. The difference is who could eventually share in the assets.

The share count is key almost as much as the treasury for investors comparing Nasdaq-listed FWDI with buying Solana directly.

The capitalization update uses a $5.53 share price and $600.2 million of net asset value (NAV). Forward's accompanying 8-K says the figures are preliminary, unaudited, and incomplete.

Dividing the same net assets among more potential shares produces three distinct comparisons:

Each discount is calculated as 1 minus the share price divided by NAV per share, using unrounded values.

The middle count already recognizes substantial claims beyond issued common stock: 12.27 million vested warrants with a displayed exercise price of $0.00, 689,010 options and 1.05 million unvested restricted stock units. It includes unvested options as well as vested ones.

Using the displayed prices for the two in-the-money option categories would add about $3.4 million if both groups vested and exercised for cash. With price, share count, and everything else fixed, that lifts the calculated discount to about 17.3%.

The largest addition between the middle and broadest counts is 13.38 million advisor and lead-investor warrants with a one-cent exercise price. Other additions include out-of-the-money options and warrants, plus 875,324 unvested performance stock units.

The accompanying prospectus to Forward's Nov. 9 filing describes three equal tranches requiring closing prices at 150%, 200% and 250% of the placement purchase price for 20 of 30 trading days after the relevant registration effectiveness.

Against the disclosed $18.50 purchase price, those trigger levels work out to $27.75, $37 and $46.25, subject to applicable adjustments.

If all 13.38 million warrants were exercised for cash at one cent, Forward would receive only about $134,000. The advisor warrant form and lead-investor warrant form also permit cashless exercise.

#5141. Bitcoin Price Trades Near $71,200 After a Fresh Session Low as the 10-Year Yield Holds Above 5%

Bitcoin price traded near $71,200 on Thursday evening, after slipping to a fresh session low near $71,000, according to Bitcoin Magazine Pro data. U.S. stocks closed modestly lower and the 10-year Treasury yield held above 5% after Nvidia said CEO Jensen Huang had resigned.

The decline started in the morning. Bitcoin price fell below $72,000 before the opening bell and dropped to about $71,150 shortly after 10 a.m. Eastern. A bounce later faded, and the bitcoin price printed a new session low near $71,000 around 6:23 p.m., its weakest level since Aug. 20. It now stands roughly $30,400 below where it closed one year ago.

The Nasdaq Composite slipped 0.39% to 25,366.09, the S&P 500 lost 0.35% to 7,592.40 and the Dow Jones Industrial Average fell 0.36% to 52,297.09, Reuters reported. Nvidia shares finished 4.3% lower at $166.19, off the day’s lows, after the company said Huang had resigned as president, chief executive and a board member, effective immediately, and named Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress interim CEO.

Bitcoin-linked stocks had a harder session. Strategy (MSTR), which MSCI is removing from its USA Index at the Nov. 30 close, finished at $114.41, down 6.5%. Shares of BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT), which had bled $2 billion over nine straight days of outflows through Wednesday, closed at $40.36, down 4.5%.

The bond market did much of the afternoon work. The 10-year Treasury yield rose about 12 basis points to 5.105% and reached 5.12%, its highest since Oct. 21, after President Donald Trump said the United States does not need a deal with Iran, Reuters reported. The two-year yield, which tracks Federal Reserve expectations most closely, climbed about 14 basis points to 4.867%. Brent crude settled at $95.82 a barrel, up 3.3%, after attacks on Saudi energy infrastructure escalated.

Higher yields reflect a market that still expects the Fed to tighten again next month. Fed funds futures are pricing in 29 basis points of hikes in December, a quarter-point move as the base case with only slim odds of a half-point step, Reuters reported. Rising rates raise the cost of holding an asset that pays no interest, and that has weighed on the bitcoin price all week.

The Treasury added a fiscal reminder after the close. The government ran a $404 billion deficit in October, the first month of fiscal 2027, the Treasury Department said, up 42% from $284 billion a year earlier, as receipts fell to $369 billion and outlays rose to $773 billion. Some benefit payments that would normally have been made on Nov. 1, a Sunday, were shifted into October.

The evening also brought a cluster of crypto headlines. The Justice Department said it has court authority to seize or restrain more than $1.2 billion of cryptocurrency stolen from Binance in October’s North Korea-linked heist, part of a freeze that officials said halted about half the breach. Binance, in a separate announcement, said it has fully covered the $6.34 billion in user losses with company funds. Jane Street’s quarterly 13F showed it had raised its Strategy stake 54% as of Sept. 30, though large options positions point to hedging rather than a straight bet. After the close, Gemini reported third-quarter revenue of $40.9 million, down 19%, as platform trading volume fell 73%; its net loss narrowed 63% to $58.6 million.

Those announcements did not lift the bitcoin price off the session lows. Friday’s Producer Price Index is the next inflation test, and Nvidia is still scheduled to report quarterly results on Nov. 17 under its interim leadership.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $71,200.

#5136. Aave user loses $305,000 after Across Protocol website hijack

Bitquery's November 12 update estimates $401,900 across nine wallets, including about $305,000 from one Aave deposit token.

A depositor in Aave lost about $305,000 in a deposit token during the Nov. 10 hijack of the cross-chain bridge Across Protocol's website, according to a Bitquery investigation updated Nov. 12.

Bitquery links the loss to spending permissions requested through the compromised interface, exposing a lending position held outside the bridge.

Bitquery's revised trace estimates $401,900 taken from nine wallets across five blockchains, using prices at the time the assets were stolen, and the Aave depositor accounts for about 76% of the traced losses. The update adds a previously missed ether sweep.

Across Protocol said its domain was taken over on Nov. 10 through social engineering involving its registrar, Namecheap.

The asset taken from the largest wallet was aWETH, an interest-bearing token representing an Aave deposit. Aave's documentation explains that its deposit tokens can be transferred and that a signed permit can authorize another account to spend them.

In Bitquery's reconstruction, the fake site requested a permit, which the attacker then used to move the depositor's tokens, and that spending permission put the separate lending position within reach.

The first transfer transaction on Nov. 10 recorded an unlimited approval followed by about 115.23 aWETH sent to one wallet and 28.81 aWETH to another. Bitquery identifies those recipients as the attacker's wallet and the drainer's fee wallet, respectively, matching an 80/20 split.

The depositor's transfer history shows four pulls ending three minutes after the first. Bitquery says the owner revoked the approval a minute after the last pull, but the transfers had already happened by that point.

#5134. OKX-ICE Tokenized Stock Venue Sets Launch, Turning to Crypto Companies After Big Tech Opts Out

OKX-ICE Tokenized Stock Venue Sets Launch, Turning to Crypto Companies After Big Tech Opts Out

After weeks of rejections from corporate America, OKX and ICE’s tokenized stock venue has found its launch lineup: crypto’s own public companies.

OKXICE said Wednesday that its Tokenized Securities Venue will open Nov. 23, letting eligible U.S. customers trade tokenized securities around the clock in the OKX app.

More than 70 assets are expected to be listed, including securities from Coinbase, Circle, Robinhood, Strategy, BitGo, Securitize and Better.com, plus 59 ETFs from Direxion. Strategy will also list its preferred stocks.

That’s a very different roster from the one the venue started with. As of its Nov. 5 notice, 67 companies, including every member of the “Magnificent Seven” tech giants, had objected to having their shares traded on the platform. Coinbase, Robinhood and Strategy were notably absent from that list, and all three are now among the venue’s launch issuers.

“The SEC has created a real opening for American markets to lead the next era of finance,” said former New York Gov. Andrew Cuomo, who co-chairs OKXICE, a joint venture between OKX and NYSE owner Intercontinental Exchange.

Securitize will provide the tokenization infrastructure. Each token represents an entitlement backed one-for-one by a real U.S. share, carrying dividends, voting rights and other shareholder benefits, according to the announcement.

Trading will run through automated market maker pools built on Uniswap v4 on X Layer, OKX’s Ethereum layer-2 network, and customers will hold their tokens in self-custodial wallets.

Strategy CEO Phong Le called tokenization on the venue “a natural next step” for the company’s preferred stocks, such as STRC.

Access is limited to eligible U.S.-based OKX customers who activate a CeDeFi Wallet, pass identity verification and complete onboarding with OKXICE. The venue operates under the SEC’s innovation exemption, which the agency unveiled in September after the Clarity Act failed to advance in the Senate. Under the exemption, companies can keep third-party tokenized versions of their stock off a given venue by objecting within 30 days.

The launch caps a busy stretch for Wall Street tokenization. DTCC’s new tokenization service, which runs partly on the Canton Network, went live in late October, and Morgan Stanley said it was among the first banks to use it.

#5132. DOJ Wins Court Authority to Seize $1.2 Billion of North Korea’s Stolen Binance Crypto as Freezes Top $2.7 Billion

The DOJ said Thursday it has court authority to seize or restrain more than $1.2 billion in cryptocurrency stolen from Binance, part of a freeze of more than $2.7 billion that officials said halted about half of North Korea’s October exchange heist.

In a Thursday announcement, the department said that on or about Oct. 29, members of the North Korean military, referred to by the FBI as TraderTraitor, stole more than $6 billion worth of virtual assets from Binance. That is below the $6.34 billion figure Binance has covered since the attack.

Binance said it had completed coverage of the assets hit in the breach, replacing the tokens from company funds rather than by recovering the stolen coins. Those are separate tracks: the DOJ is trying to interdict what was taken, while the exchange has said users were made whole from its Secure Asset Fund for Users, a portion of its futures insurance fund and its treasury.

Assistant Attorney General A. Tysen Duva of the DOJ’s Criminal Division said the department and its partners were able to “immediately halt about half of this historic breach” by freezing stolen assets “before they got beyond law enforcement reach.” The freeze of more than $2.7 billion is a joint figure for U.S. government and private-sector efforts. Of that amount, the DOJ said it has already obtained court authorization to seize or restrain more than $1.2 billion. The underlying court papers were not posted with the announcement.

The $1.2 billion is not newly recovered money but comes out of the roughly $2.85 billion Binance said on Nov. 3 had been frozen or intercepted, and Thursday’s court authorization is the legal step that lets the government move toward taking ownership of those funds through forfeiture, where until now they had simply been frozen at the token issuers.

Even that freeze is not the end of the trail. The DOJ said TraderTraitor actors are still moving the stolen assets through cryptocurrency bridges, mixers and exchanges, and that tracing, seizure and forfeiture work continues. Last week, on Oct. 31, the FBI, with the U.S. Secret Service and IRS Criminal Investigation, issued a Cyber Alert attributing the theft to North Korea and urging virtual asset service providers to block transactions with or derived from addresses the actors are using. The alert said investigators have seen the stolen assets converted primarily into ether.

Separately from the DOJ and Binance case, the U.S. attorney’s office in the Central District of California and the FBI announced the seizure of cloud-based infrastructure used in a social engineering campaign the FBI is calling FaceStealer. The FBI said the scheme has resulted in the theft of more than $400 million in cryptocurrency over the last 18 months and has also been used in attempts to compromise people who maintain open-source software, a step the bureau said appears calculated to enable supply-chain attacks.

The method is a fake video call. The FBI said North Korean hackers, known publicly under names including APT38 and BlueNoroff, lure targets with meeting invitations sent from accounts stolen from earlier victims. Once a target joins, the hackers play a prerecorded webcam video of a person the target knows, or a deepfake of a well-known figure in the cryptocurrency industry, then introduce a fake technical error and send a “fix” that installs malware. In several cases, the theft came months to more than a year after the first compromise, as the hackers waited for wallet balances to grow.

A Thursday FBI FLASH report said the meetings run on imposter versions of Zoom, Microsoft Teams and Google Meet, with invitations often moving through hijacked Telegram, LinkedIn, Slack or Discord accounts. Pursuant to a federal warrant, the FBI seized more than 900 webcam videos of FaceStealer victims. The bureau has posted a hub for the campaign at fbi.gov/facestealer.

Principal Deputy Assistant Attorney General for National Security M. Scott Proctor tied both Thursday’s actions to the same charge: “North Korea’s government bankrolls its rogue regime and illicit weapons program through cryptocurrency theft.”

The Binance recovery is still being run by the FBI’s Los Angeles Field Office, Cyber Division and Virtual Asset Unit, with the Secret Service and IRS-CI. The DOJ did not say how much of the more than $2.7 billion freeze, or of the $1.2 billion under court authority, is bitcoin.

#5131. Circle hands USDC reserves to EU regulators risking higher fees

Circle’s EU-issued USDC moves under EBA supervision, potentially raising capital, liquidity and compliance requirements.

Nov. 12 marks the transfer of supervision of EU-issued USDC’s financial safeguards to the European Banking Authority, according to Circle’s quarterly filing.

Under the disclosed arrangements, the European regulator oversees reserves and redemption planning, while France’s financial regulator keeps governance responsibilities.

For Circle’s European issuer, the change could mean more capital or liquidity held in the EU and higher compliance costs. Circle says it is evaluating the impact, without putting a figure on those potential obligations in the disclosure.

The transfer follows the EBA’s Oct. 14 classification of EU-issued USDC as a significant e-money token under the Markets in Crypto-Assets Regulation (MiCA). Circle describes USDC as the first e-money token to receive that designation.

The handover was planned since October, establishing the token’s classification. The November handover sets the effective date for the stated division of supervisory work.

The allocation applies to USDC issued in the EU by Circle Internet Financial Europe SAS, known as Circle France.

Circle says the EBA’s direct supervisory remit covers the issuer’s own funds (regulatory capital), reserve assets, and liquidity requirements. It also oversees recovery and redemption plans.

France’s Autorité de Contrôle Prudentiel et de Résolution (ACPR) remains responsible for governance, qualifying holdings, fit-and-proper assessments, outsourcing, and conflicts of interest. Circle continues to face national oversight in those areas alongside the EBA’s financial-safeguard remit.

The filing also reports an EBA memorandum of understanding with the New York State Department of Financial Services to facilitate cooperation and information exchange on cross-border stablecoin activities.

The memorandum covers cooperation and information exchange, as well as the allocation of supervisory responsibilities for the EU issuer.

Circle says the significant-token classification may bring increased regulatory capital, reserve, liquidity and other prudential requirements under MiCA. These could require additional capital or liquidity in the EU, raise compliance costs or adversely affect its business and financial results.

The disclosure provides no estimate of possible additional capital, liquidity, or compliance spending, and identifies no quantified financial charge arising on Nov. 12. Whether additional commitments will be needed, and their potential size, remain unresolved in that assessment.

Supervision of redemption plans concerns the issuer’s safeguards, and the note announces no revised cash-out terms or new holder fees. The disclosure identifies the responsible supervisors but does not specify any change to holders’ redemption arrangements.
